On the Artin formalism for triple product p p -adic L L -functions
Kâzım Büyükboduk, Ryotaro Sakamoto

Abstract
. Our main objective in the present article is to study the factorisation problem for triple-product p p -adic L L -functions, particularly in the scenarios when the defining properties of the p p -adic L L -functions involved have no bearing on this problem, although Artin formalism would suggest such a factorisation. Our analysis, which is guided by the ETNC philosophy, recasts this problem as a comparison of diagonal cycles, Beilinson–Kato elements, and Heegner cycles.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 11F66, 11F67, 11F33; Secondary 11F85, 11G18, 14F30

1.
Introduction

The principal objective of this paper (which is the first in a series of 3 papers) is to study the
irregular
factorisation problem for certain triple product
p
-adic
L
-functions as well as their algebraic counterparts. Here, the adjective
irregular
is used in reference to Perrin-Riou’s theory of
p
-adic
L
-functions, where she predicts that
p
-adic
L
-functions should come attached with what she calls regular submodules. The meaning of the adjective
irregular
is explained in §
2.4
, where we discuss how the problem at hand compares with those considered by Dasgupta in
[
Das16
]
(which is an example of a
regular
factorisation problem).

Let
f
and
\mathbf{g}
be cuspidal primitive Hida families of elliptic modular forms and let
\mathbf{g}^{c}
denote the conjugate family of
\mathbf{g}
. The main tasks we undertake can be summarized as follows.

Task 1.

We formulate a precise conjecture to factor the restriction
\mathcal{L}^{\mathbf{g}}_{p}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})_{|_{\mathcal{W}_{2}}}
of the
\mathbf{g}
-unbalanced
p
-adic triple product
L
-function
\mathcal{L}^{\mathbf{g}}_{p}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})
to a
2
-dimensional weight space
\mathcal{W}_{2}
(that we introduce in §
2.1.7
below). This factorisation conjecture reflects the Artin formalism, and addresses the scenario when the interpolation range for
\mathcal{L}^{\mathbf{g}}_{p}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})_{|_{\mathcal{W}_{2}}}
is empty.

Task 2.

We study the algebraic counterpart of this factorisation problem. This involves an extensive study of relevant Selmer complexes and what we call
modules of leading terms
.

Our Conjecture
2.1
, which settles
Task 1.
, predicts that the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}^{\mathbf{g}}_{p}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g})^{2}_{|_{\mathcal{W}_{2}}}
factors as the product of a degree-
6

p
-adic
L
-function and the image
{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}({\rm BK}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})
of Ochiai’s big Beilinson–Kato element
{\rm BK}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}
associated to
f
under a suitably defined large logarithm map
{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}
. The latter factor
{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}({\rm BK}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})
is closely related to the derivatives of
p
-adic
L
-functions; see §
6.2.4
where we elaborate on this point. The appearance of the derivatives of
p
-adic
L
-functions (albeit in disguise) in the factorisation of the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}^{\mathbf{g}}_{p}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g})^{2}_{|_{\mathcal{W}_{2}}}
is rather subtle and it is explained by what we call the “BDP-principle” (see §
2.17
, especially (
BDP)
) below).

We describe a line of attack to prove Conjecture
2.1
in full generality in Appendix
A
, through a comparison of the conjectural Gross--Kudla formulae
1
1

1

In the level of generality required in the present work, this was recently announced by Liu–Yuan–Zhang–Zhang. Since we need this result only in a degenerate case, one may also rely on
[
Xue19
]
(see especially the discussion in the final paragraph of §1.1 in op. cit.).
for diagonal cycles and Gross–Zagier formulae for Heegner cycles, and record in §
A.3.1
the key missing ingredients to complete this argument.

The algebraic counterpart of the Factorisation Conjecture
2.1
, which is the focus of
Task 2.
, is best phrased in terms of what we call the
modules of leading terms
. We introduce and study these objects in §
5
in great generality. We remark that the Selmer groups relevant to our factorisation problem will not be all torsion, and as a result, the algebraic counterpart of Conjecture
2.1
will not involve only the characteristic ideals of Selmer groups. This is unlike the scenario considered in
[
Pal18
]
(see §
2.4.2
for a relevant discussion), and it is another reflection of the
irregularity
of the factorisation problem we consider in this paper.

We establish the algebraic analogue of our Factorisation Conjecture
2.1
in great generality. Our result in this vein is recorded as Theorem
2.2
(see also Theorem
6.7
in the main body of our article). It is worth noting that
{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}({\rm BK}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})
(which encodes, as we remarked earlier, derivatives of
p
-adic
L
-functions) makes an appearance also in this statement, and it underscores the relevance of the modules of leading terms.

In our companion article
[
BC
]
, we establish further evidence for the validity of the Factorisation Conjecture
2.1
, proving it fully when
\mathbf{g}
has CM by an imaginary quadratic field
K
. In future work, we will also treat a similar problem in the context of automorphic form on
{\rm GSp}_{4}\times{\rm GL}_{2}\times{\rm GL}_{2}
.

1.1.
Layout

We provide a brief overview of the contents of our article. We introduce the notation that we frequently use in §
2.1
.
After the motivational background, we formulate our actorisation Conjecture
2.1
in §
2.2
, and we record our main results in §
2.3
. We conclude §
2
with a comparison (in §
2.4
) of the main problem that we tackle with those in the previous works
[
Gro80
,
Das16
,
Pal18
]
on related themes.

In §
3
, we review various
L
-series and
p
-adic
L
-functions that are relevant to our main problem. We introduce Greenberg-type Selmer complexes in §
4
, and study Tamagawa factors in §
4.3
(this is crucial as per our goal that our Selmer complexes are perfect).

In §
5
, we introduce a canonical cyclic submodule of an extended Selmer module, that we call the module of leading terms (of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions). The relationship between the modules of leading terms and Kolyvagin systems is explained in Remark
5.16
. Based on these constructions, we formulate and prove the algebraic counterpart of the actorisation Conjecture
2.1
in §
6
assuming that
\mathbf{g}
does not have CM.

We conclude our article with Appendix
A
, where we explain that the validity of Conjecture
2.1
can be reduced to the verification of a Generalized Gross–Kudla formula (which is the subject of the work of Liu–Yuan–Zhang–Zhang in progress) and the construction of the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm ad}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})
(see §
3.2.3
for its conjectural description).

2.
Background and results

Before stating our results in detail, we introduce in §
2.1
the notation that we will rely on throughout this work. After the preliminary discussion in §
2.2.1
–§
2.2.5
to serve as a motivation for our actorisation Conjecture
2.1
, we summarize our results in §
2.3
. We conclude this section with an extensive discussion in §
2.4
to compare the present work with earlier similar results in different settings, underlining the key technical differences and difficulties that arise in our case of interest.

2.1.
The set up

2.1.1.
Hida families

Let
p
be an odd prime and let
\mathcal{O}
be the ring of integers of a finite extension
E
of
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. Let us put
[\,\cdot\,]:\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\hookrightarrow\Lambda_{\rm wt}^{\times}
for the natural injection, where
\Lambda_{\rm wt}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}]]
. The universal weight character
\bbchi
is defined as the composite map

\bbchi:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\chi_{\textup{cyc}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\hookrightarrow\Lambda_{\rm wt}^{\times},

where
\chi_{\textup{cyc}}
is the
p
-adic cyclotomic character. A ring homomorphism
\Lambda_{\rm wt}\xrightarrow{\nu}\mathcal{O}
is called an arithmetic specialisation of weight
k\in\mathbb{Z}
if the compositum

G_{\mathbb{Q}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bbchi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Lambda_{\rm wt}^{\times}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\nu}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{O}

agrees with
\chi_{\textup{cyc}}^{k}
on an open subgroup of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
. We regard integers as elements of the weight space via

\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}\longrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{{\rm cont}}(\Lambda_{\rm wt},\mathcal{O})\,,\qquad\qquad n\longmapsto(\nu_{n}:[x]\mapsto x^{n})

For any integer
k
, let us define
\Lambda_{\rm wt}^{(k)}\cong\Lambda(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}):=\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}]]
as the “weight
k
”-eigen-subspace of
\Lambda_{\rm wt}^{(k)}
, that is,

\Lambda_{\rm wt}^{(k)}:=\{x\in\Lambda_{\rm wt}\mid[\xi]\cdot x=\xi^{k}x\,\,\textrm{ for any $\xi\in\mu_{p-1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times})$}\}.

Note that the map
\Lambda_{\rm wt}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\nu_{k}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
factors through
\Lambda_{\rm wt}^{(k)}
.
For a positive integer
N
coprime to
p
, let
\mathbf{T}^{\rm ord}(N)
denote Hida’s universal ordinary Hecke algebra of tame level
N
. We let
\textup{\bf f}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{a}_{n}(\textup{\bf f})q^{n}\in\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}[[q]]
denote the primitive Hida family of tame conductor
N_{\textup{\bf f}}
and tame nebentype character
\varepsilon_{\textup{\bf f}}
, which admits a crystalline specialisation
f_{\circ}
of weight
k
. We recall that a primitive Hida family uniquely corresponds to an irreducible component
{\rm Spec}(\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}})
of
{\rm Spec}(\mathbf{T}^{\rm ord}(N_{\textup{\bf f}}))
, whose arithmetic specialisations are new away from
p
(cf.
[
Hsi21
]
, §3.1). Here,
\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}
is the branch (i.e. the irreducible component) of Hida’s universal ordinary Hecke algebra determined by
f_{\circ}
. It is finite flat over
\Lambda_{\rm wt}^{(k)}
and one has
a_{p}(\textup{\bf f})\in\mathcal{\mathcal{R}}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\times}
.
The universal weight character
\bbchi
gives rise to the character

\bbchi_{\textup{\bf f}}:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\xrightarrow{\bbchi}\Lambda_{\rm wt}^{\times}\twoheadrightarrow\Lambda_{\rm wt}^{(k),\times}\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\times}\,.

In general, for any
\kappa\in\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}:={\rm Spf}(\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}})({\mathbb{C}}_{p})
, let us write
{\rm wt}(\kappa)\in{\rm Spf}(\Lambda_{\rm wt})({\mathbb{C}}_{p})
for the point that
\kappa
lies over and call it the weight character of
\kappa
, so that
{\rm wt}(\kappa)\circ\bbchi=\kappa\circ\bbchi_{\textup{\bf f}}
. We call
\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}
the weight space for the Hida family
f
.

We say that
\kappa\in\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}
is an arithmetic (or classical) specialisation of
f
whenever the restriction
{\rm wt}(\kappa)
of
\kappa
to
\Lambda_{\rm wt}^{(k)}
agrees with
\nu_{{\rm w}(\kappa)}
on an open subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
for some integer
{\rm w}(\kappa)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
. We denote by
\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\rm cl}\subset\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}
the set of arithmetic specialisations. We say that an arithmetic specialisation
\kappa
is crystalline if
{\rm w}(\kappa)\equiv k\pmod{p-1}
.

By the fundamental work of Hida, we have a big Galois representation

G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma}\xrightarrow{\rho_{\textup{\bf f}}}\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathrm{Frac}(\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}))
attached to
f
, where
\Sigma
is a finite set of primes containing all those dividing
pN_{\textup{\bf f}}\infty
. The Galois representation
\rho_{\textup{\bf f}}
is characterized by the property that

\mathrm{Tr}\,\rho_{\textup{\bf f}}({\rm Fr}_{\ell})=a_{\ell}(\textup{\bf f}),\qquad\forall\ell\not\in\Sigma.

Here,
{\rm Fr}_{\ell}
denotes the arithmetic Frobenius. Let us denote by
T_{\textup{\bf f}}\subset\mathrm{Frac}(\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}})^{\oplus 2}
the Ohta lattice (cf.
[
Oht99
,
Oht00
]
, see also
[
KLZ17
]
where our
T_{\textup{\bf f}}
corresponds to
M(\textup{\bf f})^{*}
in op. cit.) that realizes the Galois representation
\rho_{\textup{\bf f}}
in étale cohomology groups of a tower of modular curves. If we assume in addition that

(Irr)

the residual representation
\bar{\rho}_{\textup{\bf f}}
is absolutely irreducible

then any
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable lattice in
\mathrm{Frac}(\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}})^{\oplus 2}
is homothetic to
T_{\textup{\bf f}}
. We henceforth assume that
(Irr)
holds true for all Hida families that appear in this work. By a theorem of Wiles, we have

\rho_{\textup{\bf f}}|_{G_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}}\simeq\begin{pmatrix}\delta_{\textup{\bf f}}&*\\
0&\alpha_{\textup{\bf f}}\end{pmatrix}

where
\alpha_{\textup{\bf f}}:G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\times}
is the unramified character for which we have
\alpha_{\textup{\bf f}}({\rm Fr}_{p})=a_{p}(\textup{\bf f})
and

\delta_{\textup{\bf f}}:=\bbchi_{\textup{\bf f}}\,\chi_{\textup{cyc}}^{-1}\,\alpha_{\textup{\bf f}}^{-1}\,\varepsilon_{\textup{\bf f}}
. If we assume that

(Dist)

\delta_{\textup{\bf f}}\not\equiv\alpha_{\textup{\bf f}}\pmod{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}}}

then the lattice
T_{\textup{\bf f}}
fits in an exact sequence

(2.1)

0\longrightarrow T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{+}\longrightarrow T_{\textup{\bf f}}\longrightarrow T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{-}\longrightarrow 0

of
\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}[[G_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}]]
-modules, where the action on
T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{+}
(resp.
T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{-}
) is given by
\delta_{\textup{\bf f}}
(resp.
\alpha_{\textup{\bf f}}
).

We henceforth assume that
(Irr)
and
(Dist)
hold true for all Hida families that appear in this work.

2.1.2.
Self-dual triple products

Let
f
,
\mathbf{g}
, and
h
be three primitive Hida families of ordinary
p
-stabilized newforms of tame levels
N_{\textup{\bf f}},N_{\mathbf{g}},N_{\textup{\bf h}}
(as in §
2.1.1
), whose tame nebentype characters verify the following self-duality condition:

(2.2)

\varepsilon_{\textup{\bf f}}\varepsilon_{\mathbf{g}}\varepsilon_{\textup{\bf h}}=1.

Put
T_{3}:=T_{\textup{\bf f}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}T_{\mathbf{g}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}T_{\textup{\bf h}}
. Then
T_{3}
is a Galois representation of rank
8
over the complete local Noetherian ring
\mathcal{R}_{3}:=\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf h}}
of Krull-dimension
4
. We consider the associated weight space

\mathcal{W}_{3}:={\rm Spf}(\mathcal{R}_{3})(\mathbb{C}_{p}):=\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}\times\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{g}}\times\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf h}}.

The self-duality condition (
2.2
) ensures that we have a perfect
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant pairing

T_{3}\otimes T_{3}\longrightarrow\bbchi_{\textup{\bf f}\mathbf{g}\textup{\bf h}}\chi_{\textup{cyc}}^{-{3}},

where
\bbchi_{\textup{\bf f}\mathbf{g}\textup{\bf h}}:=\bbchi_{\textup{\bf f}}\otimes\bbchi_{\mathbf{g}}\otimes\bbchi_{\textup{\bf h}}:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathcal{R}_{3}^{\times}
.
Since
p>2
by assumption, there exists a unique character
\bbchi_{\textup{\bf f}\mathbf{g}\textup{\bf h}}^{\frac{1}{2}}:G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathcal{R}_{3}^{\times}
with
(\bbchi_{\textup{\bf f}\mathbf{g}\textup{\bf h}}^{\frac{1}{2}})^{2}=\bbchi_{\textup{\bf f}\mathbf{g}\textup{\bf h}}
. Then the Galois representation

T_{3}^{\dagger}:=T_{3}\otimes\bbchi_{\textup{\bf f}\mathbf{g}\textup{\bf h}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{\textup{cyc}}^{2}
is self-dual, in the sense that
T_{3}^{\dagger}\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{3}}(T_{3}^{\dagger},\mathcal{R}_{3})(1)
.

2.1.3.
L-functions and periods

For a crystalline specialisation

x=(\kappa,\lambda,\mu)\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\rm cl}:=\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\rm cl}\times\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{g}}^{\rm cl}\times\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf h}}^{\rm cl}

of weight
({\rm w}(\kappa),{\rm w}(\lambda),{\rm w}(\mu))
, consider the triple

f:=\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\in S_{{\rm w}(\kappa)}(N_{f},\chi_{f}),\qquad g:=\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\in S_{{\rm w}(\lambda)}(N_{g},\chi_{g}),\qquad h:=\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ}\in S_{{\rm w}(\mu)}(N_{h},\chi_{h})\,,

whose ordinary
p
-stabilisations are
(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa},\mathbf{g}_{\lambda},\textup{\bf h}_{\mu})
. Let us write

\mathscr{M}(x):=\mathscr{M}(f\otimes g\otimes h):=\mathscr{M}(f)\otimes\mathscr{M}(g)\otimes\mathscr{M}(h)

for the motive associated to
f\otimes g\otimes h
, where
\mathscr{M}(?)
(for
?=f,g,h
) stands for Scholl’s motive, whose self-dual twist is
\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}(x):=\mathscr{M}(x)(c(x))
, with

c(x):=\frac{{\rm w}(\kappa)+{\rm w}(\lambda)+{\rm w}(\mu)}{2}-1\,.

The
L
-function
L(f\otimes g\otimes h,s)
associated with the motive
\mathscr{M}(x)
is known to have an analytic continuation and under the self-duality assumption (
2.2
), it satisfies a functional equation of the form

(2.3)

\Lambda(f\otimes g\otimes h,s)\doteq\varepsilon(f\otimes g\otimes h)\cdot N(f\otimes g\otimes h)^{-s}\cdot\Lambda(f\otimes g\otimes h,2c(x)-s),

where
\Lambda(f\otimes g\otimes h,s)
denotes the completed
L
-function, with the
\Gamma
-factors
L_{\infty}(f\otimes g\otimes h,s)
, and where
N(f\otimes g\otimes h)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, and
\varepsilon(f\otimes g\otimes h)\in\{\pm 1\}
.

The study of the critical values of
L(f\otimes g\otimes h,s)
(in the sense of Deligne) shows that the set of classical points can be subdivided into the following four regions, according to the choices of periods:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\rm bal}

\displaystyle:=\{x\in\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\rm cl}\times\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{g}}^{\rm cl}\times\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf h}}^{\rm cl}\mid{\rm w}(\kappa)+{\rm w}(\lambda)+{\rm w}(\mu)>2\max\{{\rm w}(\kappa),{\rm w}(\lambda),{\rm w}(\mu)\}\}\rightsquigarrow\,\Omega^{\rm bal}\sim\langle f,f\rangle\langle g,g\rangle\langle h,h\rangle,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\textup{\bf f}}

\displaystyle:=\{x\in\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\rm cl}\times\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{g}}^{\rm cl}\times\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf h}}^{\rm cl}\mid{\rm w}(\kappa)+{\rm w}(\lambda)+{\rm w}(\mu)\leq 2{\rm w}(\kappa)\}\hskip 82.51282pt\rightsquigarrow\,\Omega^{f}\sim\langle f,f\rangle^{2},

and similarly define
\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\mathbf{g}}
and
\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\textup{\bf h}}
.

2.1.4.
p
-adic Garrett–Rankin
L
-functions

Under certain technical assumptions (cf. §
3.1
), Hsieh has constructed four
p
-adic
L
-functions

\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\bullet}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h}):\mathcal{W}_{3}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{p},\qquad\bullet\in\{{\rm bal},\textup{\bf f},\mathbf{g},\textup{\bf h}\}.

These are uniquely determined by an interpolation property of the following form: For all
(\kappa,\lambda,\mu)\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\bullet}\cap\mathcal{W}_{3}^{{\rm cl}}
, we have

(2.4)

\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\bullet}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})(\kappa,\lambda,\mu)\doteq\frac{\Lambda(f\otimes g\otimes h,c(x))}{\Omega^{\bullet}},\qquad\bullet\in\{{\rm bal},\textup{\bf f},\mathbf{g},\textup{\bf h}\}\,.

where “
\dot{=}
” means equality up to interpolation factors which we do not specify here; see Theorems
3.1
and
3.2
for the precise formulae.

2.1.5.
Root numbers and the identical vanishing of
p
-adic
L
-functions

Let us define
N:={\rm LCM}(N_{\textup{\bf f}},N_{\mathbf{g}},N_{\textup{\bf h}})
. According to Deligne, the root number
\varepsilon(f\otimes g\otimes h)
determines the parity of
\mathrm{ord}_{s=c}L(f\otimes g\otimes h,s)
at the central critical value. The root number
\varepsilon(f\otimes g\otimes h)
can be expressed as a product of local root numbers:

\varepsilon(f\otimes g\otimes h)=\prod_{v\mid N\infty}\varepsilon_{v}(f\otimes g\otimes h),\qquad\varepsilon_{v}(f\otimes g\otimes h)\in\{\pm 1\}.

The non-archimedean local root numbers are constant in families. In more precise terms,
\prod_{v\mid N}\varepsilon_{v}(f\otimes g\otimes h)
is constant as
f
,
g
and
h
vary in families thanks to the
rigidity of automorphic types
, cf.
[
FO12
, Lemma 2.14]
and
[
Dis20
, Corollary 5.3.3]
, see also the discussion on Page 414, Remark 3.1 and Definition 3.9 of
[
Hsi21
]
. On the other hand, the root number
\varepsilon_{\infty}(f\otimes g\otimes h)
at infinity is determined as follows:

\varepsilon_{\infty}(f\otimes g\otimes h)=\begin{cases}-1,&\text{if }(\kappa,\lambda,\mu)\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\rm bal},\\
+1,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

As a result,

(2.5)

\displaystyle\varepsilon(f\otimes g\otimes h)=\begin{cases}+1&\hbox{if }(\kappa,\lambda,\mu)\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\rm bal}\hbox{ and }\prod_{v\mid N}\varepsilon_{v}(f\otimes g\otimes h)=-1\\
&\hbox{or if }(\kappa,\lambda,\mu)\notin\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\rm bal}\hbox{ and }\prod_{v\mid N}\varepsilon_{v}(f\otimes g\otimes h)=+1\,,\\
\\
-1&\hbox{if }(\kappa,\lambda,\mu)\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\rm bal}\hbox{ and }\prod_{v\mid N}\varepsilon_{v}(f\otimes g\otimes h)=+1\\
&\hbox{or if }(\kappa,\lambda,\mu)\notin\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\rm bal}\hbox{ and }\prod_{v\mid N}\varepsilon_{v}(f\otimes g\otimes h)=-1\,.\\
\end{cases}

Combining the interpolation formula (
2.4
) with (
2.5
), we infer that

(2.6)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm bal}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})=0&\qquad\hbox{ if }\prod_{v\mid N}\varepsilon_{v}(f\otimes g\otimes h)=+1\,,\\
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\bullet}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})=0,\quad\bullet\in\{\textup{\bf f},\mathbf{g},\textup{\bf h}\}&\qquad\hbox{ if }\prod_{v\mid N}\varepsilon_{v}(f\otimes g\otimes h)=-1\,.\end{aligned}

In particular, the four
p
-adic
L
-functions never simultaneously assume non-zero values at crystalline specialisations.

2.1.6.

In the present paper, we are solely interested in the setting when

(2.7)

\varepsilon(f\otimes g\otimes h)=-1\quad\hbox{ for }(\kappa,\lambda,\mu)\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\rm bal}\,.

We remark that this is precisely the scenario of
[
BSV22
,
DR22
]
, where the authors have constructed cycles to account for the systematic vanishing (
2.6
) at the central critical point.

2.1.7.

Our main results concern the case when
\textup{\bf h}=\mathbf{g}^{c}
, where
\mathbf{g}^{c}=\mathbf{g}\otimes\varepsilon_{\mathbf{g}}^{-1}
is the conjugate Hida family. In this case, note that our running self-duality assumption (
2.2
) forces
\varepsilon_{\textup{\bf f}}
to be trivial.

Since
p>2
, and as
\det(\rho_{\textup{\bf f}})=\bbchi_{\textup{\bf f}}\chi_{\textup{cyc}}^{-1}
, we define the central critical twist of
T_{\textup{\bf f}}
on setting
T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}:=T_{\textup{\bf f}}\otimes\bbchi_{\textup{\bf f}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{\textup{cyc}}
. Whenever
\textup{\bf h}=\mathbf{g}^{c}
, we will identify the ring
\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf h}}
with
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
, through which we shall treat a specialisation
\lambda\in\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{g}}
of
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
also as a specialisation of
\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf h}}
. Note in this case that the specialisation
\mathbf{g}^{c}_{\lambda}
of the Hida family
\textup{\bf h}=\mathbf{g}^{c}
is the conjugate of the overconvergent eigenform
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}
: Namely,
\mathbf{g}^{c}_{\lambda}=\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}\otimes\varepsilon_{\mathbf{g}}^{-1}\,.

The Galois representation
M_{2}^{\dagger}:=T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}\,_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})
, where
{\rm ad}^{0}
denotes the trace-zero endomorphisms of
T_{\mathbf{g}}
, is then self-dual as well. We note that
M_{2}^{\dagger}
is a Galois representation of rank-
6
over the complete local Noetherian ring

\mathcal{R}_{2}:=\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
of Krull-dimension
3
.
Let us consider the associated weight space
\mathcal{W}_{2}:={\rm Spf}(\mathcal{R}_{2})(\mathbb{C}_{p})
. We decompose the set of classical points in the weight space
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\rm cl}\subset\mathcal{W}_{2}
as follows:

(2.8)

\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\rm cl}=\underbrace{\left\{(\kappa,\lambda)\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{{\rm cl}}:2{\rm w}(\lambda)>{\rm w}(\kappa)\right\}}_{\mathcal{W}_{2}^{{\rm ad}}}\quad\bigsqcup\quad\underbrace{\left\{(\kappa,\lambda)\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{{\rm cl}}:2{\rm w}(\lambda)\leq{\rm w}(\kappa)\right\}}_{\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\textup{\bf f}}}\,.

2.1.8.

Let us consider the natural homomorphisms

(2.9)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{R}_{3}&\xrightarrow{\iota_{2,3}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}&\qquad\qquad\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}&\xhookrightarrow{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\
a\otimes b\otimes c&\longmapsto a\otimes bc&\qquad\qquad a&\longmapsto a\otimes 1\end{aligned}

of complete local Noetherian
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebras, which induce the morphisms

(2.10)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{W}_{2}&\xrightarrow{\iota_{2,3}}\mathcal{W}_{3}&\qquad\qquad\mathcal{W}_{2}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varpi_{2,1}}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}\\
(\kappa,\lambda)&\longmapsto(\kappa,\lambda,\lambda)&\qquad\qquad(\kappa,\lambda)&\longmapsto\kappa\,.\\
\end{aligned}

Let us put
T_{2}^{\dagger}:=\iota_{2,3}^{*}\bigl(T_{3}^{\dagger}\bigr)
. We then have a split exact sequence

(2.11)

0\longrightarrow M_{2}^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\iota_{\rm tr}}T_{2}^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{{\rm id}\otimes{\rm tr}}\varpi^{*}_{2,1}(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\longrightarrow 0\,,

where
{\rm tr}:{\rm ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\to\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
is the trace map (which is
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-equivariant when we endow the target with trivial Galois action). The self-duality of
M_{2}^{\dagger}
,
T_{2}^{\dagger}
and
T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}
gives rise to the exact sequence

(2.12)

0\longrightarrow\varpi^{*}_{2,1}(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\xrightarrow{{\rm id}\otimes{\rm tr}^{*}}T_{2}^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{\pi_{{\rm tr}^{*}}}M_{2}^{\dagger}\longrightarrow 0\,,

where
{\rm tr}^{*}
is given by transposing the trace map and using the self-duality of
{\rm ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})
:

{\rm tr}^{*}:\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}\xrightarrow{{}^{\rm t}{\rm tr}}{\rm ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})^{*}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\,.

Both exact sequences (
2.11
) and (
2.12
) are split:

(2.13)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varpi^{*}_{2,1}(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\otimes{\rm tr}^{*}}
\textstyle{T_{2}^{\dagger}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{{\rm tr}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\otimes{\rm tr}}
\textstyle{M_{2}^{\dagger}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\rm tr}}
\textstyle{0\,.}

2.2.
The Artin formalism

Until otherwise stated, we shall work in the setting of §
2.1.7
, so that
\textup{\bf h}=\mathbf{g}^{c}
is the conjugate Hida family of
\mathbf{g}
. Recall in this scenario that we identify
\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf h}}
with
\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{g}}
.

2.2.1.

Let
x=(\kappa,\lambda,\lambda)\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{{\rm cl}}
be a classical point and let
f=\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}
,
g=\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}
and
g^{c}=\mathbf{g}^{c}_{\lambda}
denote the corresponding specialisations. The self-dual motive
\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}(x)
attached to
f\otimes g\otimes h
(cf. §
2.1.3
) then decomposes:

(2.14)

\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}(x)=(\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}(f)\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathscr{M}(g))\oplus\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}(f)\,.

The Artin formalism applied to this decomposition shows that the complex analytic Garrett–Rankin
L
-function naturally factors as

(2.15)

L(f\otimes g\otimes g^{c},s)=L(f\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}(g),s-{\rm w}(\lambda)+1)\cdot L(f,s-{\rm w}(\lambda)+1).

At the central critical point
{\rm w}(\kappa)/2+{\rm w}(\lambda)-1
, the factorisation (
2.15
) reads

(2.16)

L(f\otimes g\otimes g^{c},{\rm w}(\kappa)/2+{\rm w}(\lambda)-1)=L(f\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}(g),{\rm w}(\kappa)/2)\cdot L(f,{\rm w}(\kappa)/2)\,.

2.2.2.
p
-adic Artin formalism:
p
-adic
L
-functions of families of degree 6 and degree 2 motives.

Our main goal in the present work is to establish
p
-adic analogues of the factorisation (
2.16
). Recall that there are four
p
-adic
L
-functions one may consider, given by an interpolation formula in the range
\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\bullet}
, where
\bullet\in\{\textup{\bf f},\mathbf{g},\textup{\bf h},{\rm bal}\}
. Therefore, the
p
-adic Artin formalism in our set-up amounts to the factorisation each one of these four
p
-adic
L
-functions, into the product of two
p
-adic
L
-functions.

We briefly discuss the nature of these two
p
-adic
L
-functions to motivate the reader for the main problem at hand and for our results, even though our main results do not dwell on the constructions of these
p
-adic
L
-functions. The
p
-adic
L
-function that is relevant to the second summand in (
2.14
) (as
f
varies in the Hida family
f
) is the Mazur–Kitagawa
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm Kit}(\textup{\bf f})=\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm Kit}(\textup{\bf f})(\kappa,1+\boldsymbol{\sigma})\,,
where
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
denotes the cyclotomic variable. The putative
p
-adic
L
-functions that correspond to the first summand in (
2.14
) (as both
f
and
g
vary in the respective Hida families) are the
p
-adic
L
-functions
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{?}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})
that interpolates the algebraic parts of when
(\kappa,\lambda)\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{?}
for
?={\rm ad},\textup{\bf f}
(cf. (
2.8
)). We refer the reader to §
3.2
for details. The
p
-adic
L
-functions
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{?}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})
are presently unavailable in full generality; but as we have noted above, we do not rely on the existence of these
p
-adic
L
-functions in our main results. Since we work in the situation of (
2.7
), we have

\mathcal{L}_{p}^{{\rm bal}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})=0
owing to its interpolative properties (cf. (
2.4
)) and the functional equation (
2.3
).

2.2.3.
Root numbers

It follows from (
2.5
) and (
2.7
) that
\varepsilon(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}))=\varepsilon(\textup{\bf f})
(resp.
-\varepsilon(\textup{\bf f})
) if resp.
(\kappa,\lambda)\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\textup{\bf f}}
(resp.
(\kappa,\lambda)\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\rm ad}
), where
\varepsilon(\textup{\bf f})
is the constant value of the root number
\varepsilon(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa})
as
\kappa\in\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{{\rm cl}}
varies.

2.2.4.

In view of the interpolation formulae for the Mazur–Kitagawa
p
-adic
L
-function and the same for
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{?}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})
, we infer that either
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm ad}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})=0
(if
\varepsilon(\textup{\bf f})=+1
) , or else
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm Kit}(\textup{\bf f})(\kappa,{\rm w}(\kappa)/2)=0
identically (if
\varepsilon(\textup{\bf f})=-1
). In all cases, we have the rather uninteresting factorisation

\mathcal{L}_{p}^{{\rm bal}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})^{2}(\kappa,\lambda,\lambda)=0=\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm ad}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})\cdot L_{p}^{\rm Kit}(\textup{\bf f})(\kappa,{\rm w}(\kappa)/2)

of the balanced
p
-adic
L
-function.

2.2.5.

The factorisation of the
f
-dominant
p
-adic
L
-function

(2.17)

\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\textup{\bf f}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})^{2}(\kappa,\lambda,\lambda)\,\dot{=}\,\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\textup{\bf f}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})\cdot\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm Kit}(\textup{\bf f})(\kappa,{\rm w}(\kappa)/2)

reduces, once the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\textup{\bf f}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})
with the expected interpolative properties is constructed, to a comparison of periods at the specialisations of both sides at
(\kappa,\lambda)\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\textup{\bf f}}
. Here, “
\dot{=}
” means an equality up to a non-zero correction factor that interpolates the ratios of periods that are involved in the interpolation formulae of the
p
-adic
L
-functions that appear on two sides of (
2.17
).

We are left to consider the factorisation problem for
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})(\kappa,\lambda,\lambda)
, which is the most challenging case. To indicate the source of the difficulty, we note that

\iota_{2,3}(\mathcal{W}_{2})\cap\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\mathbf{g}}=\emptyset

and therefore, the interpolation range for
\iota_{2,3}^{*}\circ\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})
is empty. We will address this problem in the scenario when
\varepsilon(\textup{\bf f})=-1
, so that

(2.18)

\varepsilon(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}))=\begin{cases}+1&\hbox{ if }(\kappa,\lambda)\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\rm ad}\,,\\
-1&\hbox{ if }(\kappa,\lambda)\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\textup{\bf f}}\,.\end{cases}

Differentiating both sides of (
2.15
), we obtain

(2.19)

\frac{d}{ds}L(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{c},s)\big|_{s=c(x)}=L(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}(\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}),s)\big|_{s=\frac{{\rm w}(\kappa)}{2}}\cdot\frac{d}{ds}L(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa},s)\big|_{s=\frac{{\rm w}(\kappa)}{2}}\,.

Inspired by the fundamental results of
[
BDP13
,
BSV22
,
DR22
]
(and the
p
-adic Beilinson philosophy based on Perrin-Riou’s conjectures), we adopt the guiding principle that

BDP)

the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})(\kappa,\lambda,\lambda)
should be thought of as a
p
-adic avatar of the family of derivatives

\left\{\frac{d}{ds}L(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}\ \otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{c},s)\big|_{s=c(x)}\,:(\kappa,\lambda)\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\textup{\bf f}}\right\}\,.

Based on this principle, we propose the following as a
p
-adic variant of (
2.19
) in families.

Conjecture 2.1
.

Suppose that
\varepsilon(\textup{\bf f})=-1
and (
2.7
) holds true. Let
{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}({\rm BK}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})
denote the logarithm of the big Beilinson–Kato class (see §
6.1.2
for relevant definitions). We then have the following factorisation of
p
-adic L-functions:

\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})^{2}(\kappa,\lambda,\lambda)=\mathscr{C}(\kappa)\cdot\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm ad}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})(\kappa,\lambda)\cdot{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}({\rm BK}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\,,

where
\mathscr{C}\in\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}[\frac{1}{p}]
is as in §
2.2.6
.

Conjecture
2.1
is a natural variant of the factorisation problem Dasgupta studied in
[
Das16
]
for the
p
-adic Rankin–Selberg
p
-adic
L
-functions, generalising Gross’ method that he has developed in
[
Gro80
]
to study the same problem for Katz
p
-adic
L
-functions. There are, however, key technical differences, and these are detailed in §
2.4
.

See §
6.2.4
for a brief explanation as to how
{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}({\rm BK}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})
encodes information about the derivatives of
p
-adic
L
-functions. This tells us that Conjecture
2.1
is in concurrence with the principle (
BDP)
).

2.2.6.

The fudge factor
\mathscr{C}(\kappa)
(where
\kappa\in\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}
is an arithmetic specialisation) that appears in Conjecture
2.1
is explicitly defined as follows:

(2.20)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathscr{C}(\kappa):=\dfrac{w_{\textup{\bf f}}\,\cdot\mathfrak{C}_{\rm exc}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{K})\cdot\langle v_{g_{\rm Eis}}^{-},\eta_{g_{\rm Eis}}\rangle}{2\cdot(-2\sqrt{-1})^{{\rm w}(\kappa)-1}\cdot\langle\omega_{g_{\rm Eis}},v_{g_{\rm Eis}}^{+}\rangle}\times\dfrac{\omega_{K}h_{K}(1-p^{-1})\log_{p}(u)}{|D_{K}|\cdot C_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{+}C_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{-}\cdot\mathcal{E}(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ},{\rm ad})}\,.\end{aligned}

Here,
K
is an imaginary quadratic field satisfying the Heegner hypothesis relative to
f
, and,

•

w_{\textup{\bf f}}
is the Atkin–Lehner pseudo-eigenvalue for the family
f
, so that
w_{\textup{\bf f}}(\kappa)^{2}=(-N_{\textup{\bf f}})^{2-{\rm w}(\kappa)}
;

•

\mathfrak{C}_{\rm exc}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{K})=\prod_{q\in\in\Sigma_{\rm exc}}(1+q^{-1})
where
\Sigma_{\rm exc}=\Sigma_{\rm exc}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g})
is the set of primes determined by the local properties of
f
and
\mathbf{g}
(cf.
[
CH20
]
, §1.5);

•

\mathbf{g}_{K}
is the canonical CM Hida family and
g_{\rm Eis}
is its unique weight-one
p
-stabilisation as in
[
BDV22
, §4.2]
(where these are denoted by
\mathbf{g}
and
g
, respectively) ;

•

\eta_{g_{\rm Eis}}
and
\omega_{g_{\rm Eis}}
is as in
[
BDV22
, §2.3.3.1]
, and
v_{g_{\rm Eis}}^{\pm}
is defined in
[
BDV22
, §4.2]
(on Page 33);

•

h_{K}
is the class number of
K
,
\omega_{K}=|\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}|
, and
u\in\mathcal{O}_{K}[\frac{1}{p}]^{\times}
is such that
(u)=\mathfrak{p}^{h_{K}}
;

•

C_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{\pm}
is a
p
-adic period (cf.
[
Och06
]
, Theorem 6.7 and Remark 6.8).

2.3.
Results

While Conjecture
2.1
in its most general form appears to be out of reach (for reasons we explain in §
A.3.1
), we may prove the validity of its algebraic counterpart (concerning the relevant Selmer complexes). This amounts to a factorisation of the modules of leading terms (under mild hypotheses) that we introduce in §
5
.

We denote by
\delta(T,\Delta)
the module of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions for
(T,\Delta)\in\{(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}),(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\}
, given as in §
6.1.3
and §
6.1.4
, respectively. We also let
{\rm BK}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\in H^{1}(G_{\mathbb{Q}},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})
the big Beilinson–Kato class constructed by Ochiai in
[
Och06
]
.

Theorem 2.2

(Corollary
6.8
below)
.

Under the hypotheses recorded in §
6.1.1
, we have

(2.21)

\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\,{=}\,{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))\cdot\varpi_{2,1}^{*}{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}({\rm BK}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}).

2.4.
Comparison with earlier work

This subsection is dedicated to a comparison of the setting of the present work with that in
[
Das16
]
2
2

2

As well as its variant for algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions in
[
Pal18
]
), highlighting the key technical differences.

2.4.1.

Dasgupta in
[
Das16
]
relies on the factorisation

(2.22)

L^{\prime}(f\otimes f\otimes\psi\beta^{-1},1)=L({\rm Sym}^{2}f\otimes\psi\beta^{-1},1)L^{\prime}(\eta,0)

where we tacitly follow the notation of §1.4 of opt. cit. (in particular,
f
denotes here an eigenform of weight
2
). The main technical ingredients in
[
Das16
]
are the following:

D1)

Beilinson regulator formula expressing
L^{\prime}(f\otimes f\otimes\psi\beta^{-1},1)
in terms of a
Beilinson–Flach
unit
b_{f,\psi,\beta}
,

D2)

Class number formula expressing
L^{\prime}(\eta,0)
in terms of a circular unit
u_{\eta}
.

Both
b_{f,\psi,\beta}
and
u_{\eta}
live in the same one-dimensional vector space, and the input above combined with (
2.22
) yields an explicit comparison of
b_{f,\psi,\beta}
and
u_{\eta}
, which involves the critical value
L({\rm Sym}^{2}f\otimes\psi\beta^{-1},1)
.

Note that
L(f\otimes f\otimes\psi\beta^{-1},s)
has no critical values. In particular, it is not critical at
s=1
. The
p
-adic Beilinson conjectures of Perrin-Riou predicts in this case that the
p
-adic
L
-value
L_{p}(f,f,\psi,\nu_{2,\beta})
(where we still rely on the notation of
[
Das16
, §1.4]
) is related to the derivative
L^{\prime}(f\otimes f\otimes\psi\beta^{-1},1)
of the Rankin–Selberg
L
-series:

(2.23)

L_{p}(f,f,\psi,\nu_{2,\beta})\xleftrightarrow{\hbox{ $p$-adic Beilinson philosophy }}L^{\prime}(f\otimes f\otimes\psi\beta^{-1},1)\,.

The relationship (
2.23
) takes a more concrete form via
[
Das16
, Equation 18]
(which crucially relies on the work of
[
KLZ17
]
)

(2.24)

L_{p}(f,f,\psi,\nu_{2,\beta})=\log_{p}(b_{f,\psi,\beta})

combined with Step (
D1)
).

Our departure point is the factorisation (
2.19
) in the scenario when
(\kappa,\lambda)\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\rm bal}
. Let us fix crystalline specialisations
f
and
g
of
f
and
\mathbf{g}
, respectively, of respective weights
k
and
l
with
2l<k
, so that
(\kappa,\lambda)\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\textup{\bf f}}
. Note that
c(x)=\frac{k}{2}+l-1
is the central critical point of the Garrett–Rankin
L
-series
L(f\otimes g\otimes g^{c},s)
and in fact,

L(f\otimes g\otimes g^{c},s)=0
in the scenario we have placed ourselves.

In very rough terms, our guiding principle is the expected relation

(2.25)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}\to f\\
\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}\to g\\
(\kappa,\lambda)\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\mathbf{g}}\end{subarray}}L(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{c},{\rm w}(\kappa)/2+{\rm w}(\lambda)-1)^{\rm alg}\,\dot{=}\,\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(f\otimes g\otimes g^{c})&\xleftrightarrow{\ref{item_bdp1}}L^{\prime}(f\otimes g\otimes g^{c},c(x))\,,\end{aligned}

inspired by the main results of
[
BDP13
,
BSV22
,
DR22
]
(and also paralleling (
2.23
)), where the superscript “alg” denotes an appropriately defined algebraic part of the indicated
L
-value.

Note that
s=c(x)
is the central critical point, unlike the value
s=1
in (
2.22
), which is a non-critical point. The expected (
BDP)
) in (
2.25
) amounts to a combination of a) the reciprocity laws of
[
BSV22
,
DR22
]
relating diagonal cycles to
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(f\otimes g\otimes g^{c})
, and b) the Gross–Kudla conjecture expressing
L^{\prime}(f\otimes g\otimes g^{c},c(x))
in terms of the Bloch–Beilinson heights of the same cycles. Moreover, in view of the factorisation (
2.19
),
L^{\prime}(f\otimes g\otimes g^{c},c(x))
can be also computed in terms of the heights of Heegner cycles. In other words, the Artin formalism for the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(f\otimes g\otimes g^{c})
with an empty range of interpolation should amount to a comparison of a pair of Arithmetic GGP conjectures (rather than a comparison of Beilinson’s conjectures, as in the case of
[
Gro80
,
Das16
]
): One for
{\rm GL}_{2}\times{\rm GL}_{2}\times{\rm GL}_{2}
in a rather degenerate case (that boils down to Gross–Kudla conjecture), and another for
{\rm GL}_{2}
(which boils down to Gross–Zagier–Zhang formulae). We refer the reader to §
A
and
[
BCPdVP24
]
for an elaboration on this point of view.

2.4.2.

In the present subsection, we discuss the comparison of the factorisation we consider to that studied in
[
Das16
]
with the perspective offered by Perrin-Riou’s theory of
p
-adic
L
-functions.

Given a pure motive of motivic weight
w(M)\leq-2
, let us denote by
V
its
p
-adic realisation. Let
H^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q}},V)
denote the Bloch–Kato Selmer group attached to
V
and assume that the natural map

\textup{res}_{p}:\,H^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q}},V)\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},V)

is injective. In the setting we have placed ourselves, this assumption conjecturally always holds. We say that a submodule
D\subset{\bf D}_{\rm cris}(V)
is regular if

•

D\cap{\rm Fil}^{0}{\bf D}_{\rm cris}(V)=\{0\}
,

•

the natural map

D\,\,\oplus\,\,\log_{V}\,\circ\,\textup{res}_{p}(H^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q}},V))\longrightarrow{\bf D}_{\rm cris}(V)/{\rm Fil}^{0}{\bf D}_{\rm cris}(V)=:t_{V}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

is an isomorphism.

The vector space
t_{V}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is called the Bloch–Kato tangent space of
V
.

In particular, if
D\subset{\bf D}_{\rm cris}(V)
is regular, then we have

(2.26)

\dim D+\dim H^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q}},V)=\dim t_{V}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\,.

Perrin-Riou conjectures that to each pair
(V,D)
where
D\subset{\bf D}_{\rm cris}(V)
is a regular submodule, there exists a
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{p}(V,D,\sigma)
, where
\sigma
is as usual the cyclotomic variable (cf.
[
PR95
, §2 and §4]
; see also
[
Ben11
]
, §2.2.5).

We first review Perrin-Riou’s theory applied to the setting of
[
Das16
]
(where we take
\psi
to be the trivial Dirichlet character for simplicity). Let
f
be a non-CM Hida family as before and let
f=\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa_{0}}
denote a
p
-stabilized eigenform which arises as a crystalline specialisation of
f
of weight
k=w(\kappa_{0})\geq 2
. In what follows, let us put
V_{\bf\cdot}:=T_{\bf\cdot}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
to ease notation. Let us consider

(2.27)

D:={\bf D}_{\rm cris}(F^{+}V_{f}\otimes V_{f}^{*}(1))\subset{\bf D}_{\rm cris}({\rm ad}(V_{f})(1)),

where
F^{+}V_{f}\subset V_{f}
is the
p
-ordinary filtration. Note that the motivic weight of
{\rm ad}(V_{f})(1)
equals
-2
. Even though
D
is a regular submodule, the construction of
L_{p}({\rm ad}(V_{f})(1),D,\sigma)
is highly non-trivial, owing to the fact that
L({\rm ad}(f),s)
admits no critical points.

We remark that
D
can be naturally thought of as the “limit” of the sequence of reqular submodules

D_{\kappa,\lambda}:={\bf D}_{\rm cris}(F^{+}V_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}\otimes V_{\textup{\bf f}_{\lambda}}^{*}(1))\subset{\bf D}_{\rm cris}(V_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}\otimes V_{\textup{\bf f}_{\lambda}}^{*}(1))\,,\qquad{\rm w}(\kappa)>{\rm w}(\lambda)\,.

In this scenario, Hida has constructed a family of
p
-adic
L
-functions
L_{p}^{\rm Hida}({\rm ad}(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa})(1),\sigma)
. We remark that the
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{p}^{\rm Hida}({\rm ad}(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa})(1),\sigma)
coincides with the one we denoted by
L_{p}(f,f,\sigma)
in §
2.22
up to twisting, cf.
[
Das16
, §1.3]
. The work of Kings–Loeffler–Zerbes in
[
KLZ17
]
recovers Hida’s
p
-adic
L
-function as a Perrin-Riou-style
p
-adic
L
-function:

L_{p}^{\rm Hida}({\rm ad}(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa})(1),\sigma)=L_{p}({\rm ad}(V_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}})(1),D_{\kappa,\lambda},\sigma).

One then defines

L_{p}({\rm ad}(V_{f})(1),D,\sigma):=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa,\lambda\to\kappa_{0}\\
{\rm w}(\kappa)>{\rm w}(\lambda)\end{subarray}}L_{p}(V_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}\otimes V_{\textup{\bf f}_{\lambda}}^{*}(1),D_{\kappa,\lambda},\sigma)\,.

Let us consider the natural exact sequence

(2.28)

0\longrightarrow E(1)\xrightarrow{{\rm tr}^{*}}{\rm ad}(V_{f})(1)\xrightarrow{\pi_{{\rm tr}^{*}}}{\rm ad}^{0}(V_{f})(1)\longrightarrow 0\,,

induced from the transpose of the trace map and the self-duality of
{\rm ad}(V_{f})
, and where
E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is an extension that contains the Hecke field of
f
. We may
propagate
the regular subspace
D
given as in (
2.27
) to obtain the regular submodules

(2.29)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \{0\}={{\rm tr}^{*,-1}}(D)&\subset{\bf D}_{\rm cris}(E(1))\,,\\
\pi_{{\rm tr}^{*}}(D)&\subset{\bf D}_{\rm cris}({\rm ad}^{0}(V_{f})(1))\,.\end{aligned}

Thanks to the fact that
D
propagates to regular submodules of
{\bf D}_{\rm cris}(E(1))
and
{\bf D}_{\rm cris}({\rm ad}^{0}(V_{f})(1))
, one expects a factorisation

(2.30)

L_{p}({\rm ad}(V_{f})(1),D,\sigma)=L_{p}({\rm ad}^{0}(V_{f})(1),\pi_{{\rm tr}^{*}}(D),\sigma)\cdot L_{p}(E(1),\{0\},\sigma)

of Perrin-Riou-style
p
-adic
L
-functions. The factorisation (
2.30
) has been established by Dasgupta
[
Das16
]
.

The setting that we place ourselves is significantly different: The regular submodule relevant to the
g
-dominant triple product
p
-adic
L
-function associated to
f\otimes g\otimes g^{c}
does
not
propagate to regular submodules for sub-quotients. As a result, one does not expect a factorisation of Perrin-Riou-style
p
-adic
L
-functions, akin to (
2.30
), pointing towards a new phenomenon.

3.
L
-functions

In §
3
, we consider three Hida families that we shall denote by
\textup{\bf f},\mathbf{g}
and
h
, of tame levels
N_{\textup{\bf f}},N_{\mathbf{g}},N_{\textup{\bf h}}
, and tame characters
\varepsilon_{\textup{\bf f}},\varepsilon_{\mathbf{g}},\varepsilon_{\textup{\bf h}}
, respectively. For any weight space
\mathcal{W}_{?}
that will appear, we will denote by
\mathcal{W}_{?}^{\rm cris}
the subset of the classical points
in
\mathcal{W}_{?}^{\rm cl}
which are crystalline at
p
, in the sense that the
p
-adic realisation of the motive associated to
\kappa\in\mathcal{W}_{?}^{\rm cris}
is required to be crystalline at
p
. In particular, given a crystalline point
\kappa\in\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\rm cl}
of weight
{\rm w}(\kappa)\geq 2
, the specialisation
\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}
is
p
-old and arises as the unique
p
-ordinary stabilisation of a newform
\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\in S_{{\rm w}(\kappa)}(\Gamma_{1}(N_{\textup{\bf f}}),\varepsilon_{\textup{\bf f}})
(and similarly for
\mathbf{g}
and
h
).

3.1.
Garrett–Rankin (degree-8)
L
-series and interpolation of central critical values

In this subsection, we retain the notation and conventions of §
2.1.2
–§
2.1.6
.

3.1.1.

Let
f
,
\mathbf{g}
, and
h
be three primitive Hida families of ordinary
p
-stabilized newforms of respective tame levels
N_{\textup{\bf f}},N_{\mathbf{g}},N_{\textup{\bf h}}
and nebentypes
\varepsilon_{\textup{\bf f}},\varepsilon_{\mathbf{g}},\varepsilon_{\textup{\bf h}}
, all verifying the hypotheses
(Irr)
and
(Dist)
. We assume that
\varepsilon_{\textup{\bf f}}\varepsilon_{\mathbf{g}}\varepsilon_{\textup{\bf h}}
is the trivial Dirichlet character modulo
N:={\rm LCM}(N_{\textup{\bf f}},N_{\mathbf{g}},N_{\textup{\bf h}})
.

Given a
p
-crystalline classical point
x=(\kappa,\lambda,\mu)\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{{\rm cris}}:=\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\rm cris}\times\mathcal{W}_{\mathbf{g}}^{{\rm cris}}\times\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf h}}^{{\rm cris}}
, we will often write
\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ},\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ},\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ}
for the associated newforms of level prime to
p
. We will also write
c
in place of
c(x)
whenever the choice of the point
x=(\kappa,\lambda,\mu)
is clear from the context.

3.1.2.
Modified Hida periods

For any primitive Hida family
f
as before, consider the generator
\mathfrak{c}_{\textup{\bf f}}
of the congruence ideal of
f
(cf.
[
Hid88
]
). For a crystalline specialisation
\kappa\in\mathcal{W}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\rm cris}
we define the modified canonical period on setting

(3.1)

\Omega_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}:=\mathcal{E}_{p}(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ},{\rm ad})\cdot\frac{(-2\sqrt{-1})^{k+1}}{\mathfrak{c}_{\textup{\bf f}}(\kappa)}\cdot\langle\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ},\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\rangle,\qquad\text{where }\quad\mathcal{E}(\textup{\bf f}^{\circ}_{\kappa},{\rm ad}):=\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\textup{\bf f}^{\circ}_{\kappa}}}{\alpha_{\textup{\bf f}^{\circ}_{\kappa}}}\right)\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\textup{\bf f}^{\circ}_{\kappa}}}{p\alpha_{\textup{\bf f}^{\circ}_{\kappa}}}\right).

We remark that
\mathfrak{c}_{\textup{\bf f}}(\kappa):=\kappa(\mathfrak{c}_{\textup{\bf f}})
is the congruence number of
\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}
(cf.
[
Hid81
]
, (0.3)). By
[
Hid16
, Corollary 6.24 & Theorem 6.28]
, the period
\Omega_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}
is equal to the product
\Omega_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{+}\Omega_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{-}
of the plus/minus canonical periods (cf.
[
Vat99
, §1.3]
, see also
[
Hid94
, Page 488]
) up to a
p
-adic unit.

3.1.3.

Let us put

L_{\infty}(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ},s):=\begin{cases}\Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s)\ \Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s+{\rm w}(\kappa)-2c)\ \Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s+1-{\rm w}(\lambda))\ \Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s+1-{\rm w}(\mu)),&\quad x\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\textup{\bf f}},\\
\Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s)\ \Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s+1-{\rm w}(\kappa))\ \Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s+1-{\rm w}(\lambda))\ \Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s+1-{\rm w}(\mu)),&\quad x\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\rm bal}\end{cases}

(where we recall that
\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\textup{\bf f}}
and
\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\rm bal}
are defined in §
2.1.3
) and we consider then the completed motivic
L
-series

\Lambda(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ},s):=L_{\infty}(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ},s)\,L(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ},s),

where
L(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ},s)
stands for the usual motivic
L
-series given by a product of Euler factors at non-archimedean primes. The completed
L
-series
\Lambda(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ},s+c)
coincides with Garrett’s automorphic
L
-function
L(s+1/2,\Pi)
associated with the irreducible unitary automorphic triple product representation
\Pi:=\pi_{f}\times\pi_{g}\times\pi_{h}
of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})\times\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})\times\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{A})
. In particular, by the work of Piatetski-Shapiro and Rallis
[
PR87
]
, there exists
\varepsilon(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ})\in\{\pm 1\}
and a positive integer
N(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ})
such that we have a functional equation

(3.2)

\Lambda(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ},s)=\varepsilon(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ})\cdot N(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ})^{c(x)-s}\cdot\Lambda(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ},2c(x)-s)\,.

3.1.4.

We are especially interested in the central critical value
s=c
and its
p
-adic avatar. If we let
x
vary in the space
\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\rm cris}
, the algebraic parts of the central critical values are interpolated by a
p
-adic analytic
L
-function, and we note that Hsieh’s
p
-optimal construction in
[
Hsi21
]
guarantees the integrality of his
p
-adic
L
-functions.

As in
[
Hsi21
]
, we define for
x\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{?}
the modified Euler factors
\mathcal{E}_{p}^{?}(x)=\mathcal{E}_{p}^{?}(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ},c(x))

(3.3)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{E}_{p}^{?}(x)=\begin{cases}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}\alpha_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}\alpha_{\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ}}}{p^{c}}\right)^{2}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}\alpha_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}\beta_{\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ}}}{p^{c}}\right)^{2}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}\beta_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}\alpha_{\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ}}}{p^{c}}\right)^{2}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}\beta_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}\beta_{\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ}}}{p^{c}}\right)^{2}&\text{ if }?=\textup{\bf f}\\
\left(1-\frac{\alpha_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}\beta_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}\beta_{\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ}}}{p^{c}}\right)^{2}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}\alpha_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}\beta_{\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ}}}{p^{c}}\right)^{2}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}\beta_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}\alpha_{\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ}}}{p^{c}}\right)^{2}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}\beta_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}\beta_{\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ}}}{p^{c}}\right)^{2}&\text{ if }?={\rm bal}\end{cases}\end{aligned}

where
\alpha_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}
and
\beta_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}
denote the roots of the Hecke polynomial of
\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}
at
p
with
v_{p}(\alpha_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}})=0
(and we similarly define
\{\alpha_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}},\beta_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}\}
and
\{\alpha_{\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ}},\beta_{\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ}}\}
).

3.1.5.

The set

\Sigma^{-}:=\{v\mid N:\quad\varepsilon_{v}(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ})=-1,\quad\forall x\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\rm cl}\}

is well-defined. Let us denote by
\Sigma_{\mathrm{exc}}
the set of primes introduced in
[
Hsi21
, Equation (1.5)]
that only depends on the local type of the associated automorphic representations at primes dividing
N
, which is independent of
x
.

Theorem 3.1

(Hsieh)
.

Assume that
N={\rm LCM}(N_{\textup{\bf f}},N_{\mathbf{g}},N_{\textup{\bf h}})
is squarefree and that
\Sigma^{-}=\emptyset
. Then there exists a unique element
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\textup{\bf f}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})\in\mathcal{R}_{3}
with the following interpolative property for all
x=(\kappa,\lambda,\mu)\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\textup{\bf f}}
:

\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\textup{\bf f}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})(x)^{2}=\mathcal{E}_{p}^{\textup{\bf f}}(x)^{2}\cdot(\sqrt{-1})^{-2{\rm w}(\kappa)}\cdot\mathfrak{C}_{\rm exc}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})\cdot\frac{\Lambda(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ},c(x))}{\Omega_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{2}}\,,

where
\mathfrak{C}_{\rm exc}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})=\prod_{q\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{exc}}}(1+q^{-1})^{2}
.

Exchanging the roles of
f
,
\mathbf{g}
, and
h
, we analogously have the
p
-adic
L
-functions with interpolation range in the other two unbalanced regions in the weight space:
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})
and
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\textup{\bf h}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})\in\mathcal{R}_{3}
.

Theorem 3.2

(Hsieh)
.

Assume that
N={\rm LCM}(N_{\textup{\bf f}},N_{\mathbf{g}},N_{\textup{\bf h}})
is squarefree and suppose that it factors as
N=N^{+}N^{-}
, with
\gcd(N^{+},N^{-})=1
, where
N^{-}=\prod_{\ell\in\Sigma^{-}}\ell
. Assume further that
\Sigma^{-}
has odd cardinality and that all three residual representation
\overline{\rho}_{\textup{\bf f}},\overline{\rho}_{\mathbf{g}},\overline{\rho}_{\textup{\bf h}}
are ramified at all primes
\ell\in\Sigma^{-}
with
\ell\equiv 1\pmod{p}
. Then there exists a unique element
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm bal}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})\in\mathcal{R}_{3}
with the following interpolative property for all
x=(\kappa,\lambda,\mu)\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\rm bal}
:

\mathcal{L}_{p}^{{\rm bal}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})(x)^{2}=\mathcal{E}_{p}^{\rm bal}(x)^{2}\cdot(\sqrt{-1})^{1-{\rm w}(\kappa)-{\rm w}(\lambda)-{\rm w}(\mu)}\cdot\mathfrak{C}_{\rm exc}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})\cdot\frac{\Lambda(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ},c(x))}{\Omega_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}\Omega_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}\Omega_{\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}}}\,.

Notice that the period in the balanced range differs from the Gross period in
[
Hsi21
, Def. 4.12]
by an element
u\in\mathcal{R}_{3}
, which is nonzero at all classical specialisations (this difference will not make any difference for our purposes).

3.1.6.

Summarizing,
[
Hsi21
, Theorem B]
supplies us with 4
p
-adic
L
-functions

\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\textup{\bf f}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})\,,\quad\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})\,,\quad\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\textup{\bf h}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})\,,\quad\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\mathrm{bal}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})\quad\in\quad\mathcal{R}_{3}\,,

interpolating the special values of the motivic
L
-function
L(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\lambda}^{\circ}\otimes\textup{\bf h}_{\mu}^{\circ},c)
over
4
mutually disjoint ranges of interpolation in
\mathcal{W}_{3}
.

3.2.
L
-series for
f\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}(g)
and interpolation

We assume in §
3.2
that
\varepsilon_{\textup{\bf f}}=1
and retain the notation and conventions of §
3.1
, concentrating in the particular scenario when
\textup{\bf h}=\mathbf{g}^{c}:=\mathbf{g}\otimes\varepsilon_{\mathbf{g}}^{-1}
is the conjugate Hida family (as in §
2.1.7
).

3.2.1.

The point
y=(\kappa,\lambda)\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{{\rm cl}}
is called
f
-dominant if
\frac{{\rm w}(\kappa)}{2}\geq{\rm w}(\lambda)
and balanced otherwise. We define
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\textup{\bf f}}
,
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{{\rm ad}}\subset\mathcal{W}_{2}^{{\rm cl}}
as in (
2.8
).
Following Serre’s general recipe, we put

L_{\infty}(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ},s)=\begin{cases}\Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s+{\rm w}(\lambda)-1)\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s)\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s+1-{\rm w}(\lambda)),&\qquad y\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\textup{\bf f}}\,,\\
\Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s+{\rm w}(\lambda)-1)\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s)\cdot\Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s+{\rm w}(\lambda)-{\rm w}(\kappa)),&\qquad y\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\rm ad}\end{cases}

and we consider the completed motivic
L
-series

\Lambda(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ},s)=L_{\infty}(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ},s)\cdot L(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ},s),

where we define
L(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ},s)
as an Euler product of non-archimedean factors.

3.2.2.

When
y\in\mathcal{W}_{2}^{?}\subset\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\rm cris}
(
?=\textup{\bf f},{\rm ad}
), we define the modified Euler factors

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{E}^{?}_{p}(\kappa,\lambda,{\rm ad}):=\begin{cases}(1-\beta_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}\alpha_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}\beta_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}^{-1}p^{-\frac{{\rm w}(\kappa)}{2}})(1-{\beta_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}}{p^{-\frac{{\rm w}(\kappa)}{2}}})(1-{\beta_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}\beta_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}}{\alpha_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}^{-1}p^{-\frac{{\rm w}(\kappa)}{2}}}),&\text{ if }?=\textup{\bf f}\\
(1-{\alpha_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}\beta_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}}{\alpha_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}^{-1}p^{-\frac{{\rm w}(\kappa)}{2}}})(1-{\beta_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}}{p^{-\frac{{\rm w}(\kappa)}{2}}})(1-{\beta_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}}\beta_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}}{\alpha_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ}}^{-1}}p^{\frac{-{\rm w}(\kappa)}{2}}),&\text{ if }?={\rm ad}\end{cases}\end{aligned}

where
\alpha_{\bullet}
and
\beta_{\bullet}
are as in §
3.1.4
. When
\kappa
is non-crystalline, we put
\mathcal{E}^{?}_{p}(\kappa,\lambda,{\rm ad})=0
.

3.2.3.

The following conjecture is in the spirit of Hsieh’s
p
-optimal construction of triple product
p
-adic
L
-functions (which in turn dwells on the Coates–Perrin-Riou formalism):

Conjecture 3.3
.

There exist
p
-adic
L
-functions
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{?}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})\in\mathcal{R}_{2}
(where
?=\textup{\bf f},\,{\rm ad}
) with the following interpolative properties:

(3.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{p}^{?}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})(y)=\begin{cases}C_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{-}\cdot\mathfrak{g}(\psi_{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}})\cdot\mathcal{E}^{?}_{p}(\kappa,\lambda,{\rm ad})\cdot\dfrac{\Lambda(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ},\psi_{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}},\frac{{\rm w}(\kappa)}{2})}{\Omega_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{-}\cdot\Omega_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{2}}&\text{ if }?=\textup{\bf f}\,,\\
C_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{-}\cdot\mathfrak{g}(\psi_{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}})\cdot\mathcal{E}^{?}_{p}(\kappa,\lambda,{\rm ad})\cdot\dfrac{\Lambda(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}^{\circ}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}^{\circ},{\rm w}(\kappa)/2)}{\Omega_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{-}\cdot\Omega_{\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}}^{2}}&\text{ if }?={\rm ad}\,.\end{cases}

for all arithmetic
(
not necessarily crystalline
)
arithmetic specialisations
y=(\kappa,\lambda)
. Here:

•

C_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{-}
is a
p
-adic period
(
cf.
[
Och06
]
, Theorem 6.7 and Remark 6.8
)
.

•

\psi_{\kappa}
is the wild-nebentype of
\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}

(
which is trivial if
\kappa
is crystalline
)
and
\mathfrak{g}(-)
is the Gauss sum.

We note that the importance of
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm ad}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})
(whose existence is conjectural at present) in this article is due to its appearance in Conjecture
2.1
. We refer the reader to
[
Jan17
,
PdVP19
,
CdVP23
,
ACR25
]
for results towards Conjecture
3.3
(as well as the related earlier works
[
Sch88
,
Jan15
]
).

4.
Selmer complexes

We introduce in the present section the algebraic counterparts of the (
p
-adic) analytic objects we have discussed in §
3
. These will be used to formulate (and resolve) the factorisation problem for algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions. We note that our approach (that incorporates the ETNC
3
3

3

abbrv. Equivariant Tamagawa Number Conjecture.
philosophy) also sheds light on the analytic counterparts.

4.1.
Greenberg conditions

For each
T\in\{T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},T_{2}^{\dagger},T_{3}^{\dagger},M_{2}^{\dagger}\}
as in §
2.1
(with
\varepsilon_{\textup{\bf f}}=1
and
\textup{\bf h}=\mathbf{g}^{c}
, the conjugate Hida family), the fact that
f
and
\mathbf{g}
are slope-zero families, together with
(Irr)
and
(Dist)
, gives rise to short exact sequences of
G_{{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
-modules of the form

(4.1)

0\longrightarrow F^{+}T\longrightarrow T\longrightarrow T/F^{+}T\longrightarrow 0

which we shall use to define Selmer complexes. We note that even for a given
T
, there exists multiple one-step filtrations as above, which one should think of as a reflection of the fact that there are multiple choices of
p
-adic
L
-functions, depending on the chosen domain of interpolation.

Given a short exact sequence (
4.1
), we consider the following local conditions on
T
on the level of continuous cochains, in the sense of Nekovář
[
Nek06
, §6]
:

U_{v}^{+}(T)=\begin{cases}C^{\bullet}(G_{p},F^{+}T),&v=p\\
C^{\bullet}(G_{v}/I_{v},T^{I_{v}}),&v\in\Sigma\setminus\{p\}\,.\end{cases}

Here
G_{v}:=G_{\mathbb{Q}_{v}}
and
\Sigma:=\{v\mid N\}\cup\{p,\infty\}
.
These come equipped with a morphism of complexes

\iota^{+}_{v}:U_{v}^{+}(T)\longrightarrow C^{\bullet}(G_{v},T)\,,\qquad\forall v\in\Sigma\,.

For each
v\in\Sigma\setminus\{p\}
, we note that
U_{v}^{+}(T)
is quasi-isomorphic to the complex

U^{+}_{v}(T)=\left[T^{I_{v}}\xrightarrow{\,{\rm Fr}_{v}-1\,}T^{I_{v}}\right],

concentrated in degrees
0
and
1
. Here,
I_{v}\subset G_{v}
is the inertia subgroup and
{\rm Fr}_{v}\in G_{v}/I_{v}
is the (geometric) Frobenius element.

4.1.1.

Let
v\neq p
be any prime and suppose that

0\longrightarrow M_{1}\longrightarrow M_{2}\longrightarrow M_{3}\longrightarrow 0

is a short exact sequence of continuous
G_{v}
-representations, which are free of finite rank over a complete local Noetherian ring
R
. Then the sequence

0\longrightarrow U^{+}_{v}(M_{1})\longrightarrow U^{+}_{v}(M_{2})\longrightarrow U^{+}_{v}(M_{3})

is also exact. Moreover, if
M_{2}=M_{1}\oplus M_{3}
, then

(4.2)

U^{+}_{v}(M_{2})=U^{+}_{v}(M_{1})\oplus U^{+}_{v}(M_{3})\,.

4.1.2.

The data

\Delta(F^{+}T):=\{U_{v}^{+}(T)\xrightarrow{\iota_{v}^{+}}C^{\bullet}(G_{v},T):v\in\Sigma\}

is called a Greenberg-local condition on
T
. In this paragraph, we shall record examples of Greenberg local conditions, especially those crucial for our purposes.

Example 4.1
.

In this example,
X=T_{?}^{\dagger}
with
?=2,3
.

The
f
-dominant Greenberg-local condition
\Delta_{\textup{\bf f}}=\Delta(F^{+}_{\textup{\bf f}}X)
on
X
is given by the choices

\displaystyle F_{\textup{\bf f}}^{+}T_{3}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\,:=F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,T_{\mathbf{g}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}\hookrightarrow T_{3}^{\dagger}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\textup{\bf f}}^{+}T_{2}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\,:=F_{\textup{\bf f}}^{+}T_{3}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\iota_{2,3}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\,=\,F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{\rm ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\hookrightarrow T_{2}^{\dagger}\,.

The
\mathbf{g}
-dominant Greenberg-local condition
\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}:=\Delta(F^{+}_{\mathbf{g}}T_{3}^{\dagger})
on
T_{3}^{\dagger}
is given with the choice

F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}T_{3}^{\dagger}:=T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}\hookrightarrow T_{3}^{\dagger}

whereas the
\mathbf{g}
-dominant Greenberg-local condition
\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}:=\Delta(F^{+}_{\mathbf{g}}T_{2}^{\dagger})
on
T_{2}^{\dagger}
is given with the choice

F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}T_{2}^{\dagger}:=F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}T_{3}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\iota_{2,3}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}=T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\underbrace{F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}\,\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}}\,T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}}_{=:F^{+}_{\mathbf{g}}{\rm ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})}\hookrightarrow T_{2}^{\dagger}\,.

The balanced Greenberg-local condition
\Delta_{\rm bal}:=\Delta(F^{+}_{\rm bal}X)
on
X
is given with the choice

\displaystyle F_{\rm bal}^{+}T_{3}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\,:=F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}+F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,T_{\mathbf{g}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}+T_{\textup{\bf f}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}\hookrightarrow T_{3}^{\dagger}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\rm bal}^{+}T_{2}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\,:=F_{\rm bal}^{+}T_{3}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\iota_{2,3}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\,=\,F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}+F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,T_{\mathbf{g}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}}F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}+T_{\textup{\bf f}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}}F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}\hookrightarrow T_{2}^{\dagger}\,.

We will also work (especially in the proof of Proposition
6.4
) with the Greenberg local conditions
\Delta_{-}:=\Delta(F^{+}_{{\rm bal-}}X)
and
\Delta_{+}:=\Delta(F^{+}_{{\rm bal+}},X)
given by the following rank-3 and rank-5 direct summands of
X
, respectively:

\displaystyle F^{+}_{{\rm bal-}}X\,:=F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}X\cap F_{\rm bal}^{+}\,,\qquad\qquad F^{+}_{{\rm bal+}}X\,:=F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}X+F_{\rm bal}^{+}X\,.

In explicit terms,

\displaystyle F^{+}_{{\rm bal-}}T_{2}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\,:=F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}T_{2}^{\dagger}\cap F_{\rm bal}^{+}T_{2}^{\dagger}\,\,=\,\,F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}+T_{\textup{\bf f}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}}F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}\,,

\displaystyle F^{+}_{{\rm bal+}}T_{2}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\,:=F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}T_{2}^{\dagger}+F_{\rm bal}^{+}T_{2}^{\dagger}\,\,=\,\,F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}T_{2}^{\dagger}+F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,T_{\mathbf{g}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}}F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}\,.

The
\mathcal{R}_{3}
-direct-summands
F^{+}_{{\rm bal\pm}}T_{3}^{\dagger}
also have a similar explicit description, which we omit here to save ink.

Remark 4.2
.

We remark that under the self-duality pairing
T_{2}^{\dagger}\otimes T_{2}^{\dagger}\to\mathcal{R}_{2}(1)
, the local conditions
\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}
and
\Delta_{\rm bal}
are self-orthogonal-complements:

\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}^{\perp}=\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}\,,\,\,\Delta_{\rm bal}^{\perp}=\Delta_{\rm bal}\,.

Moreover,

\Delta_{+}^{\perp}=\Delta_{-},\,
and
\Delta_{-}^{\perp}=\Delta_{+}\,.

We shall propagate these local conditions to
M_{2}^{\dagger}
and
T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}
via the exact sequence (
2.12
).

Definition 4.3
.

For
?\in\{\textup{\bf f},\mathbf{g},{\rm bal}\}
, we define the Greenberg-local condition
{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{?}=\Delta(F_{?}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger})
on
M_{2}^{\dagger}
on setting

F_{?}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}:={\rm im}\left(F_{?}^{+}T_{2}^{\dagger}\hookrightarrow T_{2}^{\dagger}\twoheadrightarrow M_{2}^{\dagger}\right)\,.

Remark 4.4
.

In more explicit terms, we have put

F_{\textup{\bf f}}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}:=F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})
, and

\displaystyle F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle:=T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\underbrace{\ker\left({\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\hookrightarrow{\rm ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\to{\rm Hom}(F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}},F^{-}T_{\mathbf{g}})\right)}_{=:F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\rm bal}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle:=F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})+T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\underbrace{F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}}(F^{-}T_{\mathbf{g}})^{*}}_{=:F_{\mathbf{g}}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})}\,.

Lemma 4.5
.

We have:

{\rm rank}_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}}\,F_{\textup{\bf f}}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}\,=\,3\,=\,{\rm rank}_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}}\,F_{\rm bal}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}\,,\hbox{ and }\,{\rm rank}_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}}\,F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}\,=\,4\,.

The natural short exact sequence of
G_{p}
-representations

(4.3)

0\longrightarrow F_{\rm bal}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}\longrightarrow F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}\longrightarrow F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\longrightarrow 0\,.

Proof.

The assertions in (i) are straightforward computations and we will only explain the proof of (ii). The containment
F_{\rm bal}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}\subset F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}
is also evident and one computes that

(4.4)

{\rm coker}\left(F_{\rm bal}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}\longrightarrow F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{\rm gr}_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})

where
{\rm gr}_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}}):=F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\Big/F_{\mathbf{g}}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})
. It therefore remains to verify that

(4.5)

F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{\rm gr}_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}

as
G_{p}
-representations. This is equivalent to check that the
G_{p}
-action on
{\rm gr}_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})
is trivial.

To see that, let
\{v_{+}\}\subset F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}
denote an
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
-basis and let
\{v_{+},v_{-}\}\subset T_{\mathbf{g}}
denote a basis complementing
\{v_{+}\}
. Let us denote by
\{v_{+}^{*},v_{-}^{*}\}\subset T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}
the dual basis. Then

F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})=\ker\left({\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\hookrightarrow{\rm ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\to{\rm Hom}(F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}},F^{-}T_{\mathbf{g}})\right)=\langle v_{+}\otimes v_{-}^{*},v_{+}\otimes v_{+}^{*}-v_{-}\otimes v_{-}^{*}\rangle

and
F_{\mathbf{g}}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})=F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}}(F^{-}T_{\mathbf{g}})^{*}=\langle v_{+}\otimes v_{-}^{*}\rangle\,.
We have therefore reduced to check that

(4.6)

\left(gv_{+}\otimes gv_{+}^{*}-gv_{-}\otimes gv_{-}^{*}\right)-(v_{+}\otimes v_{+}^{*}-v_{-}\otimes v_{-}^{*})\in\langle v_{+}\otimes v_{-}^{*}\rangle\,.

Only in this proof, we let
\chi_{\pm}
denote the
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
-valued character of
G_{p}
giving its action of
F^{\pm}T_{\mathbf{g}}
. Then,

(4.7)

gv_{+}=\chi_{+}(g)v_{+}\,,\quad gv_{+}^{*}=\chi_{+}(g)^{-1}v_{+}^{*}\,,\qquad\hbox{hence }\quad gv_{+}\otimes gv_{+}^{*}=v_{+}\otimes v_{+}^{*}

and
gv_{-}=\chi_{-}(g)v_{-}+c(g)v_{+}\,\hbox{ for some }c(g)\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}\,,\quad gv_{-}^{*}=\chi_{-}(g)^{-1}v_{-}^{*}\,\,,
hence

(4.8)

gv_{-}\otimes gv_{-}^{*}=v_{-}\otimes v_{-}^{*}+c(g)v_{+}\otimes v_{-}^{*}\,.

Combining (
4.7
) and (
4.8
), we conclude that (
4.6
) holds true.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

The proof of Lemma
4.5
shows that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\mathrm{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathrm{tr^{*}}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\mathrm{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})}
\textstyle{F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\mathrm{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})/F_{\mathbf{g}}\mathrm{ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{v_{+}\otimes v_{+}^{*}-v_{-}\otimes v_{-}^{*}\,\mapsto\,1/2}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ad}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{R}_{2}.}

Definition 4.7
.

For
?\in\{\textup{\bf f},\mathbf{g},{\rm bal}\}
, we define the Greenberg-local condition
{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{?}=\Delta(F_{?}^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})
on
T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}
on setting

F_{?}^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}:={\rm ker}\left(F_{?}^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\hookrightarrow T_{2}^{\dagger}\twoheadrightarrow M_{2}^{\dagger}\right)
.

Remark 4.8
.

In more explicit form, we have set
F_{\textup{\bf f}}^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}:=F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}=:F_{\rm bal}^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}
, and
F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}=0
.
In other words, as local conditions on
T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}
, we have

{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\textup{\bf f}}=\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}={\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\rm bal}\,,\qquad{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}=\Delta_{0}\,,

where
\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}}=\Delta(F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}})
and
\Delta_{0}:=\Delta(\{0\})
.

Definition 4.9
.

We define the Greenberg local condition
\Delta_{\emptyset}
on
T
by setting
\Delta_{\emptyset}:=\Delta(T)
.

4.1.3.

We record in §
4.1.3
the definitions and various conclusions arising from the proof of Lemma
4.5
that we shall utilize later in §
6.2
.

As above, we let
\{v_{+}\}\subset F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}
denote an
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
-basis and let
\{v_{+},v_{-}\}\subset T_{\mathbf{g}}
denote a basis complementing
\{v_{+}\}
. Let us denote by
\{v_{+}^{*},v_{-}^{*}\}\subset T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*}
the dual basis. Recall that we have

F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})=\langle v_{+}\otimes v_{-}^{*},v_{+}\otimes v_{+}^{*}-v_{-}\otimes v_{-}^{*}\rangle\,,\qquad F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}=T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{+}_{\mathbf{g}}{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})\,.

Recall also the
G_{p}
-stable rank-one direct summand
F_{\mathbf{g}}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}}):=\langle v_{+}\otimes v_{-}^{*}\rangle\subset F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})
. As we have seen as part of the proof of Lemma
4.5
, the
G_{p}
acts trivially on the quotient
{\rm gr}_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}\,{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}}):=F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}})/F_{\mathbf{g}}{\rm ad}^{0}(T_{\mathbf{g}}).

4.2.
Selmer complexes associated to Greenberg local conditions

Let
T
be as in §
4.1
and let
\Delta
be a Greenberg-local condition, in the sense of Definition
4.1.2
. Let us put
