\det\left(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\big/\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)=\det\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)\,.

If in addition
\textup{res}_{p}\left(\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)\neq\{0\}
, then
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})=\{0\}
and the
\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}
-module
\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})
has rank one.

Proof.

The fact that the Selmer complexes are perfect and that they are concentrated in the indicated degrees follows from Proposition
4.39
.

Let us first prove that
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})
is free of rank
1
. Since
\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\neq 0
, the
\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}
-module
\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})
is torsion by Corollary
5.15
. The fact that
\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\in D_{\rm parf}^{[1,2]}({}_{\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}}{\rm Mod})
combined with the calculation
\chi(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset}))=-1
show that the rank of
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})
equals one. Moreover, since
R_{\textup{\bf f}}
is a 2-dimensional regular local ring,

\mathrm{Ext}_{R_{\textup{\bf f}}}^{1}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset}),M)\cong\mathrm{Ext}_{R_{\textup{\bf f}}}^{3}(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset}),M)=\{0\}

for any finitely generated
R_{\textup{\bf f}}
-module
M
. Thence,
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})
is free.

Since
\varepsilon(\textup{\bf f})=-1
, the vanishing

(6.8)

\textup{res}_{p}^{-}\left(\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)=\{0\}

follows from the comparison of the class
\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})
with the Beilinson–Kato element as in (
6.2
) and Kato’s reciprocity laws. The equality
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}})=\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})
is a consequence of the vanishing (
6.8
) and
the fact
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})
is free of rank one. This completes the proof of (i), and Part (ii) now follows from Corollary
5.15
.

Suppose now that
\textup{res}_{p}\left(\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)\neq\{0\}
. The assertion that
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})=\{0\}
follows from this assumption combined with the conclusions of (i), whereas the claim that the
\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}
-module
\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})
is of rank one follows from the calculation
\chi(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0}))=1
of the global Euler–Poincaré characteristic. Our proof is now complete.
∎

Remark 6.3
.

A conjecture of Greenberg predicts that the derivative of the Manin-Višik
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{p}(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa},\sigma)
at the central critical points is non-zero for some arithmetic specialisation
\kappa
; cf.
[
Gre94
]

(
see also
[
Arn10
]
, Conjectures 1.3 and 1.4
)
. Assuming Greenberg’s conjecture, combining with the main conjectures
(
proved by Kato, Skinner–Urban, and Wan
)
and using (
6.2
) together with Kato’s reciprocity laws, we deduce that
\textup{res}_{p}\left(\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)\neq\{0\}
.

Before we conclude §
6.1.2
, we describe a canonical trivialisation of the free module
H^{1}(G_{p},F_{p}^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})
of rank one in terms of Coleman maps. We recall that the non-anomaly hypothesis (
NA)
) is in effect.

Let us consider the unramified twist

M:=F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}(\chi_{\textup{cyc}}\bbchi_{\textup{\bf f}}^{-1})\simeq\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}(\alpha_{\textup{\bf f}}^{-1})

of the
G_{p}
-representation
F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}
, where
\alpha_{\textup{\bf f}}
is the unramified character that assigns the geometric Frobenius the value
a_{p}(\textup{\bf f})
and gives the action of
G_{p}
and
F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}
. Let us put
\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\textup{cyc}}:=\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}\widehat{\otimes}\Lambda(\Gamma_{\textup{cyc}})
to ease our notation.

The theory of Coleman maps gives rise to an isomorphism (cf.
[
KLZ17
]
, §8.2)

(6.9)

{\rm LOG}_{M,0}\,:\,H^{1}(G_{p},M\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}}\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\textup{cyc}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{D}_{\rm cris}(M)\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}}\,\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\textup{cyc}}\,,

where
\mathbf{D}_{\rm cris}(M)=(M\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{u}r}})^{G_{p}}
. Let us define the morphism

(6.10)

{\rm Log}_{F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}\,:\,H^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\longrightarrow\mathbf{D}_{\rm cris}(M)

on tensoring the isomorphism (
6.9
) of
\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\textup{cyc}}
-modules by
\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\textup{cyc}}\big/(\gamma-\chi_{\textup{cyc}}^{-1}{\chi_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}(\gamma))
(where
\gamma\in\Gamma_{\textup{cyc}}
is a topological generator)
and relying on the fact that the natural injection

H^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}}\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\textup{cyc}})\Big/(\gamma-\chi_{\textup{cyc}}^{-1}{\chi_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}(\gamma))\longrightarrow H^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})

is an isomorphism thanks to our running hypothesis (
NA)
). This in turn shows that the map
{\rm Log}_{F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}
is indeed an isomorphism.

To canonically trivialize the module
\mathbf{D}_{\rm cris}(M)
, we need Ohta’s work
[
Oht00
]
as in
[
KLZ17
, Proposition 10.1.1]
. According to op. cit., there exists a canonical isomorphism

(6.11)

\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}\,:\,\mathbf{D}_{\rm cris}(M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}

of
\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}
-modules, since the branch
f
of the Hida family we are working with is cuspidal by assumption. Combining the isomorphism (
6.10
) with (
6.11
), we obtain a canonical trivialisation

(6.12)

{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}\,:\,H^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\xrightarrow[{\rm Log}_{F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}]{\sim}\mathbf{D}_{\rm cris}(M)\xrightarrow[\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}]{\sim}\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}\,.

6.1.3.

Since we have

(6.13)

\chi(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))=-1\,,

we are in the situation of §
5.2.3
and we have a cyclic submodule

(6.14)

\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\subset\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\,.

Whenever
\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\neq\{0\}
, we have thanks to Corollary
5.15
that

(6.15)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\hbox{ is torsion-free of rank one},\quad\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\hbox{ is torsion},\\
&\det\left(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\big/\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)=\det\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)\,.\end{aligned}

On the other hand, we have

\chi(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\rm bal}))=0\,,
and we are in the situation of §
5.2.2
. We have a submodule

(6.16)

\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\rm bal})\subset\mathcal{R}_{2}

of leading terms. According to Theorem
5.18
, the submodules (
6.14
) and (
6.16
) are related to one another in the following manner. The short exact sequence (
4.3
) allows us to define the morphism

\textup{res}_{/\rm bal}:\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\longrightarrow H^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\,.

Let us fix a trivialisation
5
5

5

A canonical trivialisation of this module is given in terms of Perrin-Riou’s large dual exponential maps; see the final portion of §
6.1.2
for a prototypical example. Since we need not specify the choice of
{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}
for our purposes, we will not dwell further on this point.

(6.17)

{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}:\,H^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}

and denote also by
{\rm EXP}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}
the composite map

(6.18)

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\longrightarrow H^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\xrightarrow[{\rm EXP}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}\otimes{\rm id}]{\sim}\mathcal{R}_{2}.

Then Theorem
5.18
combined with (
4.3
) tells us that

(6.19)

{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}\left(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)=\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\rm bal})\,.

Moreover, if
{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}\left(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)\neq 0
, then it follows from Theorem
5.12
, Theorem
5.18
and (
6.19
) that

(6.20)

{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}\left(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)=\det\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\rm bal})\right)

is the module of
p
-adic
L
-functions, which is expected to be generated by the degree-6 balanced
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm ad}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})
. A generalisation of Greenberg’s conjecture leads to the prediction that
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm ad}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})\neq 0
under our assumption

(
Sign
)

on global root numbers.

We note that
\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{?})\in D_{\rm parf}^{[1,2]}({}_{\mathcal{R}_{2}}{\rm Mod})
(for
?=\mathbf{g},{\rm bal}
) and hence

(6.21)

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\rm bal})=\{0\},\quad\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\rm bal})\hbox{ is torsion}\,,

(6.22)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\longrightarrow H^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{R}_{2}\quad\hbox{ is injective}\end{aligned}

whenever
{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}\left(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)\neq\{0\}
.

6.1.4.

Note that we have
\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\in D_{\rm parf}^{[1,2]}({}_{\mathcal{R}_{?}}{\rm Mod})
thanks to Proposition
4.39
, and we have
\chi(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))=0
for the Euler–Poincaré characteristics of the indicated Selmer complex (?=2,3). We are therefore in the situation of §
5.2.2
and we have submodules

(6.23)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\subset\mathcal{R}_{3}\,,\qquad\qquad\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\subset\mathcal{R}_{2}\end{aligned}

of leading terms which are related via

\delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{3}}\mathcal{R}_{2}=\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\,,
cf. Proposition
5.11
. Whenever
\delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\neq\{0\}
, we have thanks to Theorem
5.12
that

\delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})=\det\left(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)

is the module of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions, and it is expected to be generated by the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})^{2}
, which is given as the image of a diagonal cycle under a large Perrin-Riou logarithm map.

A similar discussion applies when
\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\neq\{0\}
:

(6.24)

\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})=\det\left(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)=\det\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)

and it is expected to be generated by the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})^{2}(\kappa,\lambda,\lambda)
whose range of interpolation is empty.

Recall also the local conditions
\Delta_{+}
given as in Example
4.1
(iv). We have
\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\in D_{\rm parf}^{[1,2]}({}_{\mathcal{R}_{?}}{\rm Mod})
(where
?=2,3
) and
\chi\left(\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\right)=-1
. As a result, we are in the situation of §
5.2.3
and we have a submodule

(6.25)

\delta(T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\in\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\,,\qquad?=2,3.

We explain the manner the submodules (
6.23
) and (
6.25
) are related to one another in view of Theorem
5.18
.

Let us consider the natural map (induced by the inclusion
F^{+}_{\mathbf{g}}T_{?}^{\dagger}\longrightarrow F^{+}_{\textrm{bal}+}T_{?}^{\dagger}
, cf. Example
4.1
(iv))

(6.26)

\textup{res}^{(/\mathbf{g})}_{p}\,:\quad\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\longrightarrow{H}^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{-}T_{\mathbf{g}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*})\xrightarrow{\sim}{H}^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\mathcal{R}_{?}\,.

The canonical trivialisation (
6.12
) gives rise to a morphism

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}^{(/\mathbf{g})}}{H}^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\mathcal{R}_{?}\xrightarrow[{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}]{\sim}\mathcal{R}_{?}

which we also denote by
{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}
. Then Theorem
5.18
combined with the discussion above yields

(6.27)

{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}\left(\delta(T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\right)=\delta(T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\,.

Moreover, if
{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}\left(\delta(T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\right)\neq 0
, then it follows from Theorem
5.12
, Theorem
5.18
and (
6.27
) that

(6.28)

{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}\left(\delta(T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\right)=\delta(T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})=\det\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)

is the module of
p
-adic
L
-functions, which is expected to be generated by the degree-8 balanced
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{(\mathbf{g})}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})
(if
?=3
) and by
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{(\mathbf{g})}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})(\kappa,\lambda,\lambda)
(if
?=2
).

We further remark that we have

(6.29)

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})=\{0\},\quad\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\hbox{ is torsion}\,,

(6.30)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\hbox{ is torsion-free of rank one},\quad\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\hbox{ is torsion},\\
&\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}^{(/\mathbf{g})}}H^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}})\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\mathcal{R}_{?}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{R}_{?}\quad\hbox{ is injective}\end{aligned}

provided that
\delta(T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})={\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}\left(\delta(T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\right)\neq\{0\}
.

6.1.5.
Further consequences

We conclude §
6.1
with a non-vanishing criterion for the leading terms to supplement the discussion in §
6.1.2
–§
6.1.4
.

Similar to (
6.26
), we may also consider the natural map

\textup{res}^{(/{\rm bal})}_{p}\,:\quad\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\longrightarrow{H}^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{+}T_{\mathbf{g}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{-}T_{\mathbf{g}}^{*})\xrightarrow{\sim}{H}^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\mathcal{R}_{?}\,.

induced by the inclusion
F^{+}_{\rm bal}T_{?}^{\dagger}\longrightarrow F^{+}_{\textrm{bal}+}T_{?}^{\dagger}
, cf. Example
4.1
(iv).

The trivialisation (
6.17
) gives rise to a morphism

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}^{(/{\rm bal})}}{H}^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\mathcal{R}_{?}\xrightarrow[{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}]{\sim}\mathcal{R}_{?}

which we also denote by
{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}
. Then Theorem
5.18
combined with the discussion above yields

(6.31)

{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}\left(\delta(T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\right)=\delta(T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\rm bal})\,.

In view of the Iwasawa main conjectures and given the condition

\varepsilon(\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}\otimes\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{*}_{\mu})=-1\,,\qquad(\kappa,\lambda,\mu)\in\mathcal{W}_{3}^{\rm bal}

on the global root numbers in the scenario we have placed ourselves, we expect in light of Theorem
5.12
that

(6.32)

{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}\left(\delta(T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\right)=\delta(T_{?}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\rm bal})=\{0\}.

This is a reflection of the fact that the balanced triple product
p
-adic
L
-function vanishes identically.

Proposition 6.4
.

If

\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\neq\{0\}
,
then also
\textup{res}_{p}\left(\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)\neq\{0\}\neq\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})
. Moreover,
\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\rm bal})=\{0\}
.

Proof.

We begin by noting that the natural map

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})

is injective thanks to (
4.9
) and our running hypotheses that
(Irr)
holds for
f
and
(Irr

+

)
holds for
\mathbf{g}
. This together with (
6.29
) show that
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})=\{0\}
. We infer using duality of Selmer complexes (and the self-duality of
T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}
) that
\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})
is torsion. Theorem
5.14
tells us that
\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\neq\{0\}
. Moreover, since

\{0\}=\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})=\ker\left(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}}H^{1}(G_{p},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\right)\,,

we conclude that
\textup{res}_{p}\left(\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)\neq\{0\}
, as required.

The prove the second asserted non-vanishing, we note that the exact sequence (
4.9
) combined with the discussion in the preceding paragraph gives rise to an exact sequence

(6.33)

0\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\longrightarrow\underbrace{\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}}_{\mathrm{rank}\,=\,1}\longrightarrow\underbrace{\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})}_{\rm torsion}

of
\mathcal{R}_{2}
-modules. The exact sequence (
6.33
) then shows that
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})
is of rank one. Combining the discussion in §
5.2.3
with (
6.13
), we conclude that

\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\neq\{0\}\,.

By Remark
4.8
, we have an injection

(6.34)

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\xrightarrow{{\rm id}\otimes{\rm tr}^{*}}\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\rm bal})\,.

Since we have
\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\neq\{0\}
, it follows from Proposition
6.2
that the
\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}
-module
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}})
is free of rank one. This together with (
6.34
) show that
\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\rm bal})=\{0\}
.
∎

We will considerably strengthen Proposition
6.4
in Theorem
6.7
.

6.2.
Factorisation

Our main goal in §
6.2
is to establish Theorem
6.7
, which is one of the main results in the present article. Before doing so in §
6.2.2
, we will need to study the connecting morphism
\delta^{1}
.

The homomorphism
\mathrm{tr}\colon T_{2}^{\dagger}\longrightarrow T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}
induces a morphism

\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\longrightarrow\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\,.

Moreover, we also have a morphism (see the proof of Lemma
4.5
)

\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\longrightarrow\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{p},F_{\mathbf{g}}^{+}M_{2}^{\dagger}/F_{\mathbf{g}}M_{2}^{\dagger})\cong\widetilde{R\Gamma}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\,.

.

Then the map
\delta^{1}
factors as

6.2.1.

In §
6.2.1
, we work in the setting and under the assumptions of Corollary
6.5
and §
6.1
.
Recall our map
\textup{res}_{/{\rm bal}}
given as the composite map

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\xrightarrow{{\rm pr}_{/\mathbf{g}}\,\circ\,\textup{res}_{p}}H^{1}(G_{p},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\longrightarrow H^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\,

hence it factors as

(6.37)

\displaystyle\textup{res}_{/{\rm bal}}:\,\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{/\emptyset}}\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})}{\textup{res}_{p}(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},S},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset}))}\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}H^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\,.

Thanks to this factorisation, when
\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\neq\{0\}
, the map
\textup{res}_{/{\rm bal}}
is injective since

•

\textup{res}_{/\emptyset}
is injective,

•

\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})
is torsion-free, and

•

\ker(\pi)
is torsion. (Note that
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},S},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})=\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},S},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathrm{Pan}})
and
\mathrm{res}_{p}
is injective by Proposition
6.2
.)

Thence, we have an exact sequence of torsion
\mathcal{R}_{2}
-modules

(6.38)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} 0\longrightarrow\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}}{\textup{res}_{p}\left(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}}&\longrightarrow\dfrac{\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}}{\textup{res}_{p}\left(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}}}{\textup{res}_{/\emptyset}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\longrightarrow\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}}{{\textup{res}}_{/{\rm bal}}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\longrightarrow 0.\end{aligned}

Proposition 6.6
.

In the setting of Corollary
6.5
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm char}\left(\dfrac{\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}}{\delta^{1}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right)={\rm char}\left(\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}}{{\textup{res}}_{/{\rm bal}}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right){\rm char}\left(\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})}{\textup{res}_{p}(\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset}))}\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\right)\,.

Proof.

Combining Corollary
6.5
and (
6.38
), we infer that

\displaystyle{\rm char}\left(\dfrac{\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}}{\delta^{1}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right)

\displaystyle={\rm char}\left(\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}}{{\textup{res}}_{/{\rm bal}}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right)

(6.39)

\displaystyle\quad\times{\rm char}\left(\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})}{\textup{res}_{p}\left(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)}\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\right){\rm char}\left({\rm coker}(\partial^{1}_{\textup{\bf f}}\otimes{\rm id})\right)\,.

The long exact sequence

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise shows that
{\rm coker}(\partial_{\textup{\bf f}}^{1}\otimes{\rm id})\xrightarrow{\sim}\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}
.

) as

\displaystyle={\rm char}\left(\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}}{{\textup{res}}_{/{\rm bal}}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right){\rm char}\left(\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})}{\textup{res}_{p}(\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset}))}\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\right)\,,

as required.
∎

6.2.2.

We are now ready to explain our factorisation result concerning algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions, whenever the relevant leading terms are non-trivial.

Theorem 6.7
.

Under the hypotheses recorded in §
6.1.1
, we have

(6.40)

{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}\left(\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{+})\right)=\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\,{=}\,{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))\cdot\varpi_{2,1}^{*}{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}(\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})).

In particular, if
\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\neq\{0\}
, then
{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))=\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\rm bal})\neq\{0\}\neq{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}(\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset}))
.

Proof.

The first equality in (
6.40
) is (
6.27
).
Let us prove the second equality. Suppose first that
\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})=\{0\}
. We may assume without loss if generality that
\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\neq\{0\}\neq{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}(\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset}))
, since otherwise the claimed equality (
6.40
) reduces to
0=0
. These assumptions imply that

\widetilde{H}_{\rm f}^{1}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})
is a non-trivial torsion-free
\mathcal{R}_{2}
-module by Remark
5.13
,
that
\widetilde{H}_{\rm f}^{1}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})
is torsion-free of rank
1
by (
6.15
),
and that
\widetilde{H}_{\rm f}^{1}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})=0
by Proposition
6.2
(iii).
Hence, it follows from the exact triangle (
4.9
) that the morphism
\widetilde{H}_{\rm f}^{1}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\hookrightarrow\widetilde{H}_{\rm f}^{1}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})
is injective and its cokernel is a torsion
\mathcal{R}_{2}
-module.
Consequently, by (
6.35
) and (
6.37
),
the homomorphism
\textup{res}_{/{\rm bal}}
is zero.
In particular,

{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))={\rm char}\left(\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}}{{\textup{res}}_{/{\rm bal}}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right)=\{0\}.

This concludes the proof of (
6.40
) when
\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})=\{0\}
. Next, suppose that
\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\neq\{0\}
. It follows on combining (
4.9
) and Proposition
6.4
that the sequence

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} 0\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\xrightarrow{\delta^{1}}\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\rightarrow 0\end{aligned}

is exact. This shows that

(6.41)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\rm char}&\left(\widetilde{H}^{1}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\big/\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)\,{\rm char}\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad={\rm char}\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\big/\delta^{1}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))\right){\rm char}\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)\,.\end{aligned}

Combining (
6.41
) with (
6.15
), we conclude that

(6.42)

{\rm char}\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)={\rm char}\left(\frac{\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}}{\delta^{1}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right)\,.

This equality together with Proposition
6.6
then yields

(6.43)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\rm char}\left(\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\rm f}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\right)\,&={\rm char}\left(\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}}{{\textup{res}}_{/{\rm bal}}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))}\right){\rm char}\left(\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})}{\textup{res}_{p}(\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset}))}\otimes_{\varpi_{2,1}^{*}}\mathcal{R}_{2}\right)\\
&={\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))\varpi_{2,1}^{*}{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}(\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset}))\,.\end{aligned}

The proof of our theorem is now complete on combining (
6.43
) with (
6.24
).
∎

Corollary 6.8
.

Under the hypotheses recorded in §
6.1.1
, we have

\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})\,{=}\,{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))\cdot\varpi_{2,1}^{*}{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}({\rm BK}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}).

Proof.

This corollary follows from Theorem
6.7
together with (
6.2
).
∎

6.2.3.

As we have noted in §
6.1.4
and §
6.1.3
, we expect (in view of Iwasawa–Greenberg main conjectures) that the module
\delta(T_{2}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathbf{g}})
of leading terms is generated by
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\mathbf{g}}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\mathbf{g}^{c})^{2}(\kappa,\lambda,\lambda)
, whereas the module
{\rm Exp}_{F^{-}T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger}}^{*}(\delta(M_{2}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathbf{g}}))
is expected to be generated by the degree-6 balanced
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm ad}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}\mathbf{g})
.

Moreover, recall that
\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})
can be chosen as the Beilinson–Kato element (constructed by Ochiai in this setup).
Very loosely speaking, the element
{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}(\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset}))\in\mathcal{R}_{\textup{\bf f}}
then interpolates the derivatives of Perrin-Riou’s
\mathbf{D}_{\rm cris}(V_{f}^{*})\otimes\mathcal{H}(\Gamma_{\textup{cyc}})
-valued
p
-adic
L
-functions attached to crystalline members
f
of the family
f
at their central critical points, paired with the canonical vector
\omega_{f}\in{\rm Fil}^{1}\mathbf{D}_{\rm cris}(V_{f})
(cf.
[
PR93
]
, §2.2.2).

The precise relation of
\delta(T_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})
with
p
-adic
L
-functions will be explained in §
6.2.4
, and it will be clear that (
6.40
) is indeed an algebraic analogue of the factorisation predicted by Conjecture
2.1
.

6.2.4.

Let us assume that
\kappa
is a crystalline specialisation and
f=f_{\kappa}
. Let
f_{0}
denote the newform associated to
f
, and let us denote by
\alpha_{0},\beta_{0}
the roots of its Hecke polynomial at
p
. We assume that
v_{p}(\alpha_{0})=0
and
f_{0}^{\beta_{0}}
is non-theta-critical. Combining
[
PR93
, §2.2.2]
with Kato’s reciprocity laws
[
Kat04
, Theorem 16.6]
(as enhanced in
[
BB22
]
to cover the case of critical-slope
p
-adic
L
-functions),
[
KLZ17
, Proposition 10.1.1(1)]
, and the interpolative properties of the big logarithm map given as in (
6.9
), we deduce that

(6.44)

{\rm Log}_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}}}({\rm BK}_{\textup{\bf f}}^{\dagger})(\kappa)=d_{\alpha}L_{p,\alpha_{0}}^{\prime}(f_{0},{w(\kappa)}/{2})+d_{\beta}L_{p,\beta_{0}}^{\prime}(f_{0},{w(\kappa)}/{2})\,,\qquad d_{\alpha},d_{\beta}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}\,.

Appendix A
A strategy to prove Conjecture

We outline an action plan to prove a weak form

6

3.4
) (with
?={\rm ad}
).

2.1.2.

\mathscr{L}\in\mathcal{R}_{2}
.

Once (i) is verified, the verification of (ii) follows once the
p

as in Conjecture

alluded to above is (

) above.

Thanks to the fundamental results in

K
where
p
splits and that verifies the Heegner hypothesis relative to
f
(so that we have a ring isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{N}\simeq\mathbb{Z}/N_{\textup{\bf f}}\mathbb{Z}

of

. A natural recourse
7
7

7

This action plan is, of course, akin to the strategy of (Gross and) Dasgupta we outlined in §
2.4
. One fundamental difference is that one needs to compare cycles, as opposed to elements in the motivic cohomology groups of non-critical motives (or rather, their realisations in Deligne cohomology). Since these cycles come about to explain the derivatives of
L
-functions at the central critical point, one needs to work with complex analytic heights in the present setting.
to prove Conjecture

, therefore, is to establish a comparison between diagonal cycles and Heegner cycles. Such a relationship will come about as a consequence of Gross–Kudla conjecture and the Gross–Zagier–Zhang formulae for Heegner cycles. We note that the diagonal cycles and Heegner points live in different spaces (the former in the Chow group of codimension-
2
cycles on the product of
3
modular curves, the latter in the Jacobian of a modular curve), hence they have no chance to admit a direct comparison. It is the “Chow–Heegner points” of Darmon–Rotger–Sols
[
DRS12
]
(which are the images of diagonal cycles under an intersection pairing) that play an intermediary role; see §

below for their key properties.

From this standpoint, (

) can reformulated as follows

, and
L_{s_{\kappa}}=H_{p^{s_{\kappa}}}(\mu_{p^{s_{\kappa}}})
.

•

Q_{\kappa}\in H^{1}(K,T_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{\dagger})
is Howard’s twisted Heegner point given as in
[

, §2.2.2]
.

•

\mathscr{D}(\kappa):=\dfrac{2^{2{\rm w}(\kappa)-1}(-1)^{{\rm w}(\kappa)+1}}{\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm Kit}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\epsilon_{K})(\kappa,\frac{{\rm w}(\kappa)}{2})}\times\dfrac{\mathfrak{C}_{\rm exc}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})}{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{\kappa}(\mathfrak{N}^{-1})(\sqrt{-1})^{\frac{{\rm w}(\kappa)}{2}-1}\sqrt{-D_{K}}}=\dfrac{-8\cdot\mathfrak{C}_{\rm exc}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\mathbf{g}\otimes\textup{\bf h})}{\mathcal{L}_{p}^{\rm Kit}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes\epsilon_{K})(\kappa,1)\cdot\boldsymbol{\xi}_{\kappa}(\mathfrak{N}^{-1})\sqrt{-D_{K}}}

), and:

Note that we implicitly assume that
\log_{\omega_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}}(Q_{\kappa})\neq 0
for all but finitely many
(\kappa,\lambda)
as above. While this is conjectured by Greenberg to be always the case, we may assume without loss of generality that this is indeed the case; cf.
[
BCPdVP24
, §7.2.1]
.

A.1.1.

The discussion in Section
A.1
tells us that one may directly formulate Conjecture
2.1
in terms of the logarithm of a big Heegner point (relative to an auxiliary choice of an imaginary quadratic field) rather than the big Beilinson–Kato class. However, based on our analysis with the algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions and modules of leading terms in §
6
, we were led to believe that it is more natural to formulate this conjecture in terms of the logarithm of a big Beilinson–Kato element.

A.2.
Reformulation of (

2.1.1
′

.
) in terms of Chow–Heegner points

We retain the notation in §
A.1
. Let us fix throughout this subsection
(\kappa,\lambda)
as in (

2.1.1
′

.
), let us put
f=\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N_{\textup{\bf f}})\cap\Gamma_{1}(p^{s_{\kappa}}),\psi_{\kappa})
and
g=\mathbf{g}_{\lambda}\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{0}(N_{\mathbf{g}})\cap\Gamma_{1}(p^{s_{\kappa}}))
to ease our notation. Let us denote by
J
the Jacobian of the compactified modular curve of level
\Gamma_{0}(N_{\textup{\bf f}})\cap\Gamma_{1}(p^{s_{\kappa}})
.

Following
[
YZZ10
, §1.4]
and
[
DRS12
, §2]
, we define the Chow–Heegner point
P^{\circ}(\kappa,\lambda)\in(J(L_{s_{\kappa}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}})^{\psi_{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}}}
as in
[
BCPdVP24
, §5.2.1]
. By a slight abuse, we denote the image of
P^{\circ}(\kappa,\lambda)
in
H^{1}_{\rm f}(L_{s_{\kappa}},T_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{\dagger}[\frac{1}{p}])^{\psi_{\kappa}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\simeq H^{1}_{\rm f}(K,T_{\textup{\bf f}_{\kappa}}^{\dagger}[\frac{1}{p}])
under the Kummer map also by
P^{\circ}(\kappa,\lambda)
, where the isomorphism follows from the inflation-restriction sequence (cf.
[
BCPdVP24
]
, §7.2.1). As explained in Corollary 6.3 of op. cit. (based on the ideas in Daub’s thesis
[
Dau13
]
), we have

\log_{\omega_{f}}(\Delta^{\rm(tr)}_{p}(\textup{\bf f}\otimes{\rm ad}(\mathbf{g}))_{|_{\kappa,\lambda}})=a_{p}(g)^{s_{\kappa}}\,\log_{\omega_{f}}\circ\,\textup{res}_{p}\,(P^{\circ}(\kappa,\lambda))\,.

We may therefore reformulate (

2.1.1
′

.
) as follows:

\mathbf{CH\,vs\,H}
.

We have

P^{\circ}(\kappa,\lambda)=\pm a_{p}(g)^{-\frac{s_{\kappa}}{2}}a_{p}(f)^{-s_{\kappa}}p^{1-s_{\kappa}}[L_{s_{\kappa}}:H_{p^{s_{\kappa}}}]^{-1}{\mathscr{R}}_{\kappa,\lambda}\eqref{eqn_conj_2022_06_02_0940}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{D}(\kappa)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot Q_{\kappa}\qquad\hbox{ in }\quad J(L_{s_{\kappa}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}

for some choice of
\pm\in\{+,-\}
.

We refer the reader to
[
BCPdVP24
, Proposition 6.5]
where it is explained that (

⁢
CH
vs
H

.
) is indeed equivalent to (

2.1.1
′

.
).

A.3.
Generalized Gross–Kudla conjecture and heights of Chow–Heegner points

We retain the notation and conventions of §
A.1
and §
A.2
. In order to prove (

⁢
CH
vs
H

.
), the key ingredients are:

\mathbf{I.1}
.

The twisted Gross–Zagier theorems of Howard
[
How09
, Theorem 5.6.2]
(see also
[
BCPdVP24
]
, Theorem 7.1), expressing the derivative
L^{\prime}(f,\psi_{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}},1)
in terms of the Néron–Tate height of
Q_{\kappa}\in J(L_{s_{\kappa}})
.

\mathbf{I.2}
.

The Generalized Gross–Kudla conjecture (
[
BCPdVP24
]
, Conjecture 5.1) expressing the derivative
L^{\prime}(f\otimes g\otimes g^{c},\psi_{\kappa}^{-\frac{1}{2}},2)
in terms of Bloch–Beilinson heights of diagonal cycles.

\mathbf{I.3}
.

Comparison of the Bloch–Beilinson heights of diagonal cycles and the Néron–Tate heights of the twisted Heegner points
Q_{\kappa}
(cf.
[
YZZ12
, §1.3.1]
; see also
[
YZZ23
, Remark 3.1.1]
and
[
BCPdVP24
]
, §5.2.1).

A.3.1.

The only missing ingredient to complete the proof of (

⁢
CH
vs
H

.
), therefore, is (

⁢
I
.2

.
). This is the subject of the preprints of Yuan–Zhang–Zhang
[
YZZ10
,
YZZ12
,
YZZ23
]
. Note that the results of these articles do not cover the required level of generality (as they require that the levels of all factors are square-free) to establish
[
BCPdVP24
, Conjecture 5.1]
. However, it is expected that a generalisation of the results in these preprints by Liu--Yuan--Zhang--Zhang (as announced by S. Zhang) will settle
9
9

9

Note that we need this result only in a degenerate case (i.e. when
h=g^{c}
). In this scenario, one can alternatively attempt to establish (

⁢
I
.2

.
) using the results of
[
Xue19
]
(see especially the discussion in the final paragraph of §1.1 in op. cit.), but we have not pursued this. We remark that
[
Xue19
]
there are no restrictions on the levels since one works in op. cit. over the generic fibre.
(

⁢
I
.2

.
), and together with the circle of ideas sketched in this Appendix (as detailed in our companion article
[
BCPdVP24
]
), it will complete the proof of (
2.1.1.
).

A.3.2.

In (

2.1.1
′

.
), we work only with specialisations of weight
2
. This is because, if
{\rm w}(\kappa)>2
, the analogous comparison to what is predicted by (

⁢
CH
vs
H

.
) would take place in the Chow group of homologically trivial codimension-
\frac{{\rm w}(\kappa)}{2}
cycles on the suitable Kuga–Sato variety (rather than the Jacobian of the appropriately defined modular curve). Even if the higher-weight generalisation of the Gross–Kudla conjecture were available (which currently seems out of reach), one would also need the injectivity of a
p
-adic Abel–Jacobi map (which is possibly a much harder problem than Conjecture
2.1
).
